\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\frac{|k|(\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(0)-\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(0))}{(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(0))(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(0))}\cdot\frac{1}{{\rm i}}v^{(2)}_{+}(0)r_{1}(0)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{d\tau_{+}}\underbrace{\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(0)}\left[-v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)v^{(1)}_{+}(0)+v^{(2)}_{+}(0)v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)\right]}_{=-\tau_{+}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}v^{(2)}_{+}(t)s(t)dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{d\tau_{-}}\left[\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(0)}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)v^{(2)}_{-}(0)-\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(0)}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(0)}v^{(2)}_{+}(0)v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)\right]\int\limits_{-\infty}^{0}v^{(1)}_{-}(t)s(t)dt.

Next we bound
A_{+},A_{-}
with respect to their
k
-dependency. First, using the embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C_{b}(\mathbb{R})
(with embedding constant
C_{0}
) and that
r_{1}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}k_{2}r_{2}
for
N=2
and
r^{\prime}_{1}=-{\rm i}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})
for
N=3
, we find

\displaystyle|r_{1}(0,k)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\|r_{1}(\cdot,k)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}=C_{0}\sqrt{\|r_{1}(\cdot,k)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}+\|r^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot,k)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}}

(3.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\sqrt{\|r_{1}(\cdot,k)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}+|k|^{2}(\|r_{2}(\cdot,k)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}+\|r_{3}(\cdot,k)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}.

Furthermore, using the assumptions ii), and (
3.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}v^{(2)}_{+}(t)s(t)dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{v,2,+}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\nu_{+}t}|s(t)|dt\leq C_{v,2,+}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\nu_{+}}}\|s\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

(3.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{v,2,+}c\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\nu_{+}}}\frac{1}{1+|k|^{2}}\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}},

and analogously,

\left|\int\limits_{-\infty}^{0}v^{(1)}_{-}(t)s(t)dt\right|\leq C_{v,1,-}c\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\nu_{-}}}\frac{1}{1+|k|^{2}}\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}.

(3.20)

Using assumptions i), ii), and (
3.16
) - (
3.20
), we find

\displaystyle|A_{+}(k)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{|d|}\frac{c}{1+|k|^{3}}C_{v,1,-}C_{0}\sqrt{1+|k|^{2}}\right.

(3.21)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{|d||\tau_{+}|}\left[\frac{c}{1+|k|^{2}}\left(C_{v,1,-}C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,1,+}\right)\right]\frac{C_{v,2,+}c}{\sqrt{2\nu_{+}}(1+|k|^{2})}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{d}\frac{C_{v,1,-}c}{\sqrt{2\nu_{-}}(1+|k|^{2})}\right\}\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{|d|(1+|k|^{2})}\left\{C_{v,1,-}+\frac{C_{v,1,-}C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,1,+}}{|\tau_{+}|(1+|k|^{2})}\frac{C_{v,2,+}}{\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}+\frac{C_{v,1,-}}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right\}\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}.

Analogously

\displaystyle|A_{-}(k)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{|d|(1+|k|^{2})}\left\{C_{v,2,+}+\frac{C_{v,2,+}}{\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,2,-}+C_{v,2,+}C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}}{|\tau_{-}|(1+|k|^{2})}\frac{C_{v,1,-}}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right\}\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}.

(3.22)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|A_{+}(k)v^{(2)}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{+}(k)|C_{v,2,+}\|e^{-\nu_{+}\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}=|A_{+}(k)|\frac{C_{v,2,+}}{\sqrt{2\nu_{+}}}

(3.23)

\displaystyle\hskip-155.0pt\leq

\displaystyle\hskip-85.0pt\frac{cC_{v,2,+}}{|d|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\left\{C_{v,1,-}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right)+\frac{\left(C_{v,1,-}C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,1,+}\right)C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right\}\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}.

Since, by assumption iv), the factor
c\alpha_{2}^{+}
in (
3.23
) is a bounded function of
k
, we obtain some global constant
c
such that

A_{+}v^{(2)}_{+}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N}),\|A_{+}v^{(2)}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.24)

Analogously,

A_{-}v^{(1)}_{-}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}^{N}),\|A_{-}v^{(1)}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.25)

Together with (
3.9
), (
3.10
), we find

v\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.26)

Next we show in an analogous way that also

\frac{v^{\prime}}{1+|k|}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\left\|\frac{v^{\prime}}{1+|k|}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.27)

Indeed, using (
3.12
) and assumptions ii) we obtain

\displaystyle|v_{+}^{\prime}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{+}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)|+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,1,+}}{|\tau_{+}|}e^{-\nu_{+}x}\int\limits_{0}^{x}e^{\nu_{+}t}|s(t)|dt+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|}e^{\nu_{+}x}\int\limits_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\nu_{+}t}|s(t)|dt\quad(x>0),

and hence, by Lemma 4.7 in
[
7
]
,

\displaystyle\|v^{\prime}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq|A_{+}|C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}\|e^{-\nu_{+}\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|\nu_{+}}\|s\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}.

Using (
3.21
) and (
3.8
), we conclude

\displaystyle\|v^{\prime}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\frac{c\penalty\ C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}}{|d|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\left\{C_{v,1,-}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right)+\frac{(C_{v,1,-}C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,1,+})C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|\nu_{+}}\frac{c}{1+|k|^{2}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}},

which gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+|k|}\|v^{\prime}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(1+|k|^{3})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\left[\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}}{|d|}\bigg\{C_{v,1,-}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right)+\right.\

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{(C_{v,1,-}C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,1,+})C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\bigg\}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}C_{v,2,+}}{|\tau_{+}|\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}.

Since the factor
c\alpha_{3}^{+}
on the right-hand side is bounded independently of
k
by assumption iv), we conclude

\frac{1}{1+|k|}v^{\prime}_{+}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N}),\qquad\left\|\frac{1}{1+|k|}v^{\prime}_{+}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{+})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.28)

Analogously, (
3.12
), Lemma 4.7 in
[
7
]
, (
3.22
) and (
3.8
) imply

\displaystyle|v^{\prime}_{-}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{-}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(x)|+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}C_{v,1,-}}{|\tau_{-}|}e^{-\nu_{-}x}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\nu_{-}t}|s(t)|dt

\displaystyle+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,2,-}}{|\tau_{-}|}e^{\nu_{-}x}\int\limits_{x}^{0}e^{-\nu_{-}t}|s(t)|dt\quad(x<0),

\displaystyle\|v^{\prime}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{-}|C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}\|e^{\nu_{-}\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}C_{v,1,-}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,2,-}}{|\tau_{-}|\nu_{-}}\|s\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\frac{c\penalty\ C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}}{|d|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\left\{C_{v,2,+}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right)+\frac{(C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,2,-}+C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}C_{v,2,+})C_{v,1,-}}{|\tau_{-}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}C_{v,1,-}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v,2,-}}{|\tau_{-}|\nu_{-}}\frac{c}{1+|k|^{2}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})^{3}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+|k|}\|v^{\prime}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{(1+|k|^{3})\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\left[\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}}{|d|}\left\{C_{v,2,+}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right)+\frac{(C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}C_{v,2,-}+C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}C_{v,2,+})C_{v,1,-}}{|\tau_{-}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\frac{C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}C_{v,1,-}+C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}C_{v^{(2)}_{-}}}{|\tau_{-}|\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})^{3}}.

The factor
c\alpha_{3}^{-}
on the right-hand side is bounded independently of
k
by assumption iv), and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+|k|}v^{\prime}_{-}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}^{N}),\left\|\frac{1}{1+|k|}v^{\prime}_{-}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{-})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}^{N})^{3}},

which together with (
3.28
) gives (
3.27
).

Now we show the remaining properties of
u_{1}
and
v
required in the definition of
\mathcal{\widetilde{D}}_{\omega}
.

By (
2.17
) and (
3.27
) (and assumption i)), we obtain

u_{1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\penalty\ \|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.29)

Rearranging (
2.17
) gives

v^{\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u_{1}=-\frac{\omega^{2}\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}-\frac{{\rm i}|k|}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}r_{1},

(3.30)

and

\displaystyle{\rm i}|k|v^{\prime}+|k|^{2}u_{1}=-\frac{{\rm i}|k|\omega^{2}\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}+\frac{|k|^{2}}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}r_{1}

which are both in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
by (
3.27
). Furthermore, (
3.30
), (
2.15
), and
r^{\prime}_{1}+{\rm i}k_{2}r_{2}+{\rm i}k_{3}r_{3}=0
imply for
N=3

\displaystyle v^{\prime\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{\omega^{2}\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{{\rm i}|k|}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}r^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{{\rm i}|k|\omega^{2}\perm^{\prime}}{(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)^{2}}r_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\perm v+\frac{\omega^{2}\perm}{|k|(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})-\frac{|k|}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\perm v-\frac{1}{|k|}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})

which is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
by (
3.26
). For
N=2
the same calculation but with
k_{3}=0
produces

\displaystyle v^{\prime\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\perm v-\mbox{\rm sign}(k_{2})r_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

Moreover, by (
2.17
), (
2.15
) and using
r_{1}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})
for
N=3
,

\displaystyle(\perm u_{1})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k|\perm v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}(-{\rm i}|k|v^{\prime}+r_{1})\right]^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k|\perm v

(3.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}|k|\left[-\left(\frac{\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}+\perm v\right]+\frac{|k|^{2}\perm^{\prime}}{(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)^{2}}r_{1}+\frac{\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}r_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}|k|\left[\frac{{\rm i}|k|\perm^{\prime}}{(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)^{2}}r_{1}+\frac{\perm}{|k|(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{|k|^{2}\perm^{\prime}}{(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)^{2}}r_{1}-\frac{{\rm i}\perm}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\quad\rm{\penalty\ on\penalty\ }\mathbb{R}_{\pm}.

For
N=2
the same calculation applies if we set
k_{3}=0
.

Moreover,
u_{1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
by (
3.29
) and thus
u_{1}(\cdot,k)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
and
\perm u_{1}(\cdot,k)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
for almost all
k
. Therefore, since (
3.31
) gives
(\perm u_{1}(\cdot,k))^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
, and
\llbracket\perm u_{1}\rrbracket=0
by (
3.14
), we obtain
\perm u_{1}(\cdot,k)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.

Furthermore,
(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)u_{1}=-{\rm i}|k|v^{\prime}+r_{1}
by (
2.17
), implying

\displaystyle v^{\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u_{1}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}|k|}(\omega^{2}\perm u_{1}+r_{1})\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}),

and, since
\llbracket\perm u_{1}\rrbracket=\llbracket r_{1}\rrbracket=0
, we have

\llbracket v^{\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u_{1}\rrbracket=0.

Finally,
\llbracket v\rrbracket=0
by (
3.11
), which completes the proof of all properties concerning
u_{1}
and
v
in
\mathcal{\widetilde{D}}_{\omega}
.

To prove also the properties concerning
w
in
\mathcal{\widetilde{D}}_{\omega}
, let
\widetilde{s}
denote the right-hand side of (
2.16
). With
\{w^{(1)}_{+},w^{(2)}_{+}\}
and
\{w^{(1)}_{-},w^{(2)}_{-}\}
denoting the fundamental systems of problem (
2.16
) given by assumption iii), we proceed as before for
v
, and obtain (
3.6
) and (
3.7
), now with
w_{\pm}^{\rm{(part)}}
instead of
v_{\pm}^{\rm{(part)}}
and with all expressions on the right-hand side correspondingly replaced, i.e.,
v_{\pm}^{(1,2)},s,\tau_{\pm}
replaced by
w_{\pm}^{(1,2)},\widetilde{s},\widetilde{\tau}_{\pm}
respectively. We also obtain (
3.8
) for
\widetilde{s}
, but without the denominator
1+|k|^{2}
.

Hence, the boundedness of
\alpha_{4}^{\pm}
in assumption iv) implies (
3.9
) for
(1+|k|)w_{\pm}^{\rm{(part)}}
, and we obtain the general solution in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
of problem (
2.16
) by

\begin{array}[]{ll}w_{+}(x)=\widetilde{A}_{+}w^{(2)}_{+}(x)+w_{+}^{\rm{(part)}}(x)&(x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}),\\
w_{-}(x)=\widetilde{A}_{-}w^{(1)}_{-}(x)+w_{-}^{\rm{(part)}}(x)&(x\in\mathbb{R}_{-}),\end{array}

(3.32)

with (
k
-dependent) constants
\widetilde{A}_{+},\widetilde{A}_{-}\in\mathbb{C}
. The condition
\llbracket w\rrbracket=0
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
gives equation (
3.11
), and
w^{\prime}_{\pm}
is given by (
3.12
) (both with the corresponding replacements).

The condition
\llbracket w^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
therefore requires

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{+}w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)-\widetilde{A}_{-}w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)=\frac{w_{+}^{(1)^{\prime}}(0)}{\widetilde{\tau}_{+}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}w^{(2)}_{+}(t)\widetilde{s}(t)dt-\frac{w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)}{\widetilde{\tau}_{-}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{0}w^{(1)}_{-}(t)\widetilde{s}(t)dt.

Together with (
3.11
) (with the corresponding replacements), we obtain a
2\times 2
system for
(\widetilde{A}_{+},\widetilde{A}_{-})^{\top}
with determinant
\widetilde{d}=-w^{(2)}_{+}(0)w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)+w^{(1)}_{-}(0)w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)
which is non-zero by assumption iii). Thus,
\widetilde{A}_{+}
and
\widetilde{A}_{-}
are uniquely calculated as

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widetilde{d}}\left[\frac{w^{(1)}_{-}(0)w_{+}^{(1)^{\prime}}(0)-w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)w^{(1)}_{+}(0)}{\widetilde{\tau}_{+}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}w^{(2)}_{+}(t)\widetilde{s}(t)dt-\int\limits_{-\infty}^{0}w^{(1)}_{-}(t)\widetilde{s}(t)dt\right],

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widetilde{d}}\left[-\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}w^{(2)}_{+}(t)\widetilde{s}(t)dt+\frac{w^{(2)}_{-}(0)w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)-w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)w^{(2)}_{+}(0)}{\widetilde{\tau}_{-}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{0}w^{(1)}_{-}(t)\widetilde{s}(t)dt\right].

Using analogues of (
3.19
) and (
3.20
) (again, the denominator
1+|k|^{2}
is not present now), we estimate

|\widetilde{A}_{+}|\leq\frac{c}{|\widetilde{d}|}\left[\frac{C_{w,1,-}C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}C_{w,1,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|}\cdot\frac{C_{w,2,+}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}+\frac{C_{w,1,-}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}},

(3.33)

|\widetilde{A}_{-}|\leq\frac{c}{|\widetilde{d}|}\left[\frac{C_{w,2,+}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}+\frac{C_{w,2,-}C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,2,-}C_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|}\frac{C_{w,1,-}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}

(3.34)

with some
k
-independent constant
c
. Consequently, as
\|w^{(2)}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\frac{C_{w,2,+}}{\sqrt{2\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}
and
\|w^{(1)}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}\leq\frac{C_{w,1,-}}{\sqrt{2\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}
, we get

\displaystyle(1+|k|)\|\widetilde{A}_{+}(k)w^{(2)}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\frac{c\penalty\ C_{w,2,+}(1+|k|)}{|\widetilde{d}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}\left[\frac{C_{w,1,-}C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}C_{w,1,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}C_{w,2,+}+\frac{C_{w,1,-}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}

and

\displaystyle(1+|k|)\|\widetilde{A}_{-}(k)w^{(1)}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}\leq\frac{c\penalty\ C_{w,1,-}(1+|k|)}{|\widetilde{d}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\left[\frac{C_{w,2,+}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}+\frac{C_{w,2,-}C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,2,-}C_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}C_{w,1,-}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}}.

Since, by assumption iv), the two factors
c\alpha_{5}^{\pm}
on the right-hand sides are bounded independently of
k
, and since (
3.9
) holds for
(1+|k|)w_{\pm}^{\rm{(part)}}
, we obtain from (
3.32
) that

(1+|k|)w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\|(1+|k|)w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}},

(3.35)

which shows that both
w
and
|k|w
are in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. The next task is to show that
w^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. Indeed, using (
3.12
) (with the corresponding replacements, see above) and assumption ii) we find

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}_{+}(x)|\leq|\widetilde{A}_{+}|C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}C_{w,1,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|}e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\int\limits_{0}^{x}e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}t}|\widetilde{s}(t)|dt+\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}C_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|}e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\int\limits_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}t}|\widetilde{s}(t)|dt\ (x>0),

whence Lemma 4.7 in
[
7
]
and (
3.33
) give

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\widetilde{A}_{+}|\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}}{\sqrt{2\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}C_{w,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}C_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}\|\widetilde{s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\frac{c\penalty\ C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{d}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}\left\{\frac{C_{w,1,-}C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}C_{w,1,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}C_{w,2,+}+\frac{C_{w,1,-}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+c\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}C_{w,1,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}C_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}.

Since the factor
c\alpha_{6}^{+}
on the right-hand side is bounded independently of
k
by assumption iv), we conclude

w^{\prime}_{+}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{+}),\penalty\ \|w^{\prime}_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.36)

Analogously,

\displaystyle|w^{\prime}_{-}(x)|\leq|\widetilde{A}_{-}|C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}x}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,-}C_{w,1,-}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|}e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{-}x}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{x}e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}t}|\widetilde{s}(t)|dt+\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}C_{w,2,-}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|}e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}x}\int\limits_{x}^{0}e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{-}t}|\widetilde{s}(t)|dt,

\displaystyle\|w^{\prime}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\widetilde{A}_{-}|\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}}{\sqrt{2\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}+\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,-}C_{w,1,-}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}C_{w,2,-}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}\|\widetilde{s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\frac{c\penalty\ C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}}{|\widetilde{d}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\left\{\frac{C_{w,2,+}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}+\frac{C_{w,2,-}C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}+C^{\prime}_{w,2,-}C_{w,2,+}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}C_{w,1,-}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+c\frac{C^{\prime}_{w,2,-}C_{w,1,-}+C^{\prime}_{w,1,-}C_{w,2,-}}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{-}|\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}\right]\|r(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})^{3}}.

Again, the factor
c\alpha_{6}^{-}
on the right-hand side is bounded independently of
k
by iv), and hence

\displaystyle w^{\prime}_{-}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{-}),\penalty\ \|w^{\prime}_{-}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}^{N})^{3}},

which together with (
3.36
) implies

w^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\penalty\ \|w^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}.

(3.37)

Finally, by (
2.16
),

\displaystyle w^{\prime\prime}-|k|^{2}w=\begin{cases}-\omega^{2}\perm w-\mbox{\rm sign}(k_{2})r_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\penalty\ &{\rm if}\penalty\ N=2,\\
-\omega^{2}\perm w-\frac{1}{|k|}(k_{2}r_{2}+k_{3}r_{3})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\penalty\ &{\rm if}\penalty\ N=3,\end{cases}

which completes the proof of
(u_{1},v,w)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
, and (
3.26
), (
3.29
), (
3.35
) imply

\displaystyle\left\|(u_{1},v,w)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq c\|r\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}},

and hence
\omega\in\rho(\mathcal{L})
.
∎

3.1
Media Homogeneous in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}

While in
[
7
]
we considered the one- and two-dimensional cases with homogeneous media on either side of the interface, in this section we analyze the 3D case in the same situation
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)=\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
for those
\omega\in\mathbb{C}
for which

\omega\notin\Omega_{0}=\{\omega\in D(\perm):\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)=0\quad\text{or}\quad\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(\omega)=0\}.

(3.38)

Of course, the case of
\perm_{\pm}
independent of
x_{1}
is a special case of periodic
\perm_{\pm}
studied in Sec.
3.2
. However, as the calculations and results for the homogeneous case are very explicit, we present them here independently.

We recall the definition of the sets

\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0}:\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)\neq 0,\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}^{2}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)},\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{-}^{2}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}\notin[0,\infty),\right.

(3.39)

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 80.0pt\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}\in(0,\infty)\right\}

and

{\mathcal{M}}_{\pm}^{\mathrm{red}}=\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0}:\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\omega)\in(0,\infty)\}

(3.40)

from Theorem
B
. We show that the complement of the union of theses sets and of
\Omega_{0}
lies in
\mathcal{S}
and therefore in the resolvent set.

Proposition 3.3
.

Assume
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)=\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
. Then

D(\perm)\setminus({\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{+}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{-}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup\Omega_{0})\subset\mathcal{S}.

Proof.

We first define for each
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
the set

\displaystyle N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0},|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega),\ |k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(\omega)\notin(-\infty,0],\right.

(3.41)

\displaystyle\left.\ \text{and}\ \omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)=|k|^{2}(\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega))\right\}

and claim that

\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}={\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}.

(3.42)

Note that these sets will be also used in Section
4
, where for
\omega\in N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}
we will
generate Weyl sequences localized at the interface, while for
\omega\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\pm}^{\mathrm{red}}
Weyl sequences with their support moving to infinity in the
x_{1}
-direction will be found.

To show (
3.42
), first note that for any fixed
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
and any
\omega\in N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}
we have
\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)\neq 0
because otherwise the last equation in (
3.41
) implies
\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)=0
, i.e.,
\omega\in\Omega_{0}
. Solving this equation for
|k|^{2}
, we get
|k|^{2}=\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}
. Hence, if
\omega\in N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}
for some
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
, then

\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}\in\mathbb{R}_{+},

(3.43)

and

\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}-\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\omega)\notin(-\infty,0]

for both cases
+
and
-
. The value
0
is excluded in (
3.43
) due to
\omega\notin\Omega_{0}
. After simplification, we get the conditions in the definition of
{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}
. This shows
\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}
.

For the opposite inclusion, we take
\omega\in{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}
and choose some
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
with
|k|^{2}=\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}
. Then, clearly,
\omega\in N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}
and (
3.42
) follows.

Next, we proceed to show the inclusion in the statement of the proposition. We show that i)-iv) in Definition
3.1
are satisfied by
\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus({\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{+}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{-}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup\Omega_{0})
.

Condition i) is satisfied as
\omega\notin{\mathcal{M}}_{+}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{-}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup\Omega_{0}
and
\perm_{\pm}
is independent of
x
. For ii) and iii) first note that equations (
2.15
) and (
2.16
) are identical if
r=0
and
\perm_{\pm}
are independent of
x
. For a given
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
a fundamental system suitable for ii) is

v^{(1)}_{+}(x)=e^{\mu_{+}x},v^{(2)}_{+}(x)=e^{-\mu_{+}x}\quad\text{and}\quad v^{(1)}_{-}(x)=e^{\mu_{-}x},v^{(2)}_{-}(x)=e^{-\mu_{-}x},

where

\mu_{\pm}:=\sqrt{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\omega)}.

Because
\omega\notin{\mathcal{M}}_{\pm}^{\mathrm{red}}
, we have
\mathop{\rm Re}(\mu_{\pm})>0
. Recall the definition of the complex square root from Sec.
1.1
. As a result,
v^{(1)}_{+}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
and
v^{(2)}_{-}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
.

The estimates in ii) hold with

\nu_{\pm}:=\mathop{\rm Re}(\mu_{\pm}),\ C_{v,1,\pm}=C_{v,2,\pm}=1\ \text{and}\ C_{v,1,\pm}^{\prime}=C_{v,2,\pm}^{\prime}=|\mu_{\pm}|.

For
d
we get

d=-\omega^{2}\left(\frac{\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\mu_{-}}+\frac{\perm_{+}(\omega)}{\mu_{+}}\right)\neq 0

because
\omega\notin\Omega_{0}
and because the expression in the parentheses vanishes if and only if
\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)=|k|^{2}(\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega))
, i.e. only if
\omega\in N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}
, see Remark 4.6 in
[
7
]
.

As explained above, for iii) we can choose

w^{(1)}_{\pm}=v^{(1)}_{\pm},\ w^{(2)}_{\pm}=v^{(2)}_{\pm},\ C_{w,1,\pm}=C_{v,1,\pm},\ C_{w,2,\pm}=C_{v,2,\pm},\ C^{\prime}_{w,1,\pm}=C^{\prime}_{v,1,\pm},\ C^{\prime}_{w,2,\pm}=C^{\prime}_{v,2,\pm},\text{ and }\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}=\nu_{\pm}.

For
\widetilde{d}
we obtain

\widetilde{d}=-(\mu_{+}+\mu_{-})\neq 0

because
\mathop{\rm Re}(\mu_{\pm})>0.

It remains to show that the constants
\alpha_{j}^{\pm},j=1,\dots,6
, are bounded independently of
k
. First note that

\tau_{\pm}=-2\omega^{2}\frac{\perm_{\pm}}{\mu_{\pm}}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\tau}_{\pm}=-2\mu_{\pm}.

The following straightforward estimates are used below

\displaystyle\nu_{\pm}\gtrsim 1+|k|,\ 1+|k|\lesssim|\mu_{\pm}|\lesssim 1+|k|,\ |\perm_{-}\mu_{+}+\perm_{+}\mu_{-}|\gtrsim 1+|k|,

\displaystyle|d|,|\tau_{\pm}|\gtrsim(1+|k|)^{-1},\ \text{and}\ |\widetilde{d}|,|\widetilde{\tau}_{\pm}|\gtrsim 1+|k|,

where
\lesssim
and
\gtrsim
denote inequalities up to
k
-independent multiplicative constants. Their straightforward application produces

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|\omega|^{2}|\perm_{\pm}|}\frac{|\mu_{\pm}|}{\nu_{\pm}(1+|k|^{2})}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu_{\pm}(1+|k|)}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|^{2}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|d|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}+\frac{|\mu_{+}|+|\mu_{-}|}{|\tau_{+}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|^{3/2}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{3})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\left[\frac{|\mu_{+}|}{|d|}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu_{-}}}+\frac{|\mu_{+}|+|\mu_{-}|}{|\tau_{+}|(1+|k|^{2})\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right)+\frac{|\mu_{+}|+|\mu_{-}|}{|\tau_{+}|\sqrt{\nu_{+}}}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|^{3/2}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+|k|}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{\pm}|\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|},

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+|k|}{|\widetilde{d}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}\left(\frac{|\mu_{+}|+|\mu_{-}|}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|},

\displaystyle\alpha_{6}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\mu_{+}|}{|\widetilde{d}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}}\left(\frac{|\mu_{+}|+|\mu_{-}|}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widetilde{\nu}_{-}}}\right)+\frac{|\mu_{+}|+|\mu_{-}|}{|\widetilde{\tau}_{+}|\widetilde{\nu}_{+}}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|},

and analogously

\alpha_{2}^{-}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|^{3/2}},\quad\alpha_{3}^{-}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|^{3/2}},\quad\alpha_{5}^{-},\alpha_{6}^{-}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|k|}.

∎

3.2
Media Periodic in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}

Here we consider the case of
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)
being
a_{\pm}
-periodic with
a_{\pm}>0
. Clearly, equation (
2.16
) for the component
w
has the form of the Schrödinger equation (
2.36
) if we set
V=\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}
. But also equation (
2.15
) for the component
v
transforms to (
2.36
) if we set
V=\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}-\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{\perm_{\pm}^{\prime}}{\perm_{\pm}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\perm_{\pm}^{\prime\prime}}{\perm_{\pm}}
, as we showed in Lemma
2.3
. Therefore, results of Sec.
2.2
for the periodic Schrödinger equation play a central role here.

In the case when the discriminant of the differential equation (
2.15
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
does not lie in
[-2,2]
, the fundamental system
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
from (
2.35
) can be chosen as the fundamental system
v^{(1)}_{+}
and
v^{(2)}_{+}
in Def.
3.1
. This is because in this case
\Re(\kappa)>0
in (
2.35
).

Definition 3.4
.

For
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)
being
a_{\pm}
-periodic with
a_{\pm}>0
we define the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{p}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega:D_{+}^{(v)}(k),D_{-}^{(v)}(k),D_{+}^{(w)}(k),D_{-}^{(w)}(k)\notin[-2,2],\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad d(k)\neq 0\quad\hbox{ and }\quad\widetilde{d}(k)\neq 0\quad\hbox{ for all }k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\hbox{ and}\quad\perm_{+}(0,\omega)+\perm_{-}(0,\omega)\neq 0\quad\text{or}\quad\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0,\omega)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0,\omega)\neq 0\right\},

where
D_{\pm}^{(v)}(k)
and
D_{\pm}^{(w)}(k)
denote the discriminants of the homogeneous differential equations (
2.15
) and (
2.16
), respectively, on the intervals
[0,a_{+}]
and
[-a_{-},0]
according to the index
\pm
, respectively.
The quantities
d
and
\widetilde{d}
were introduced in Definition
3.1
.

In view of Lemma
2.3
one may wonder whether it makes a difference if, in Definition
3.4
, we consider the discriminants
D_{\pm}^{(v)}(k)
based on the homogeneous Sturm-Liouville equation (
2.15
) (i.e. (
2.22
)) for
v
or the equivalent homogeneous Schrödinger equation (
2.23
) for

z=\frac{\sqrt{\omega^{2}\perm}}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime};

(3.44)

see Lemma
2.3
. The next lemma shows that this is not the case.

Lemma 3.5
.

The monodromy matrices
\Phi_{\pm}(a_{\pm})
and
\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}(a_{\pm})
of the differential equations (
2.22
) and (
2.23
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
, respectively, are related via
\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}(a_{\pm})=T_{\pm}\Phi_{\pm}(a_{\pm})T_{\pm}^{-1}
, where

T_{\pm}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\frac{\omega^{2}}{\sqrt{\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(0)}}\\
\sqrt{\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(0)}&-\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}^{\prime}(0)}{2\perm_{\pm}(0)\sqrt{\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(0)}}\end{array}\right).

As a consequence, the eigenvalues (and thus the discriminants) of
\Phi_{\pm}(a_{\pm})
and
\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}(a_{\pm})
coincide.

Proof.

Using the notation
W_{\pm}=\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)
, the fundamental matrix
\Phi_{\pm}(x)
of (
2.22
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
satisfying
\Phi_{\pm}(0)=I
is of the form

\Phi_{\pm}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}v^{(1)}_{\pm}&v^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
\frac{W_{\pm}}{\omega^{2}\left(|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}\right)}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\frac{W_{\pm}}{\omega^{2}\left(|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}\right)}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\end{array}\right)

with suitably normalised fundamental solutions
v^{(1)}_{\pm}
,
v^{(2)}_{\pm}
. Noting Remark
2.4
, by (
3.44
), a fundamental matrix of (
2.23
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}z^{(1)}_{\pm}&z^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
z^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&z^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\\
\left(\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{\perm_{\pm}}\frac{\perm_{\pm}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\right)^{\prime}&\left(\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{\perm_{\pm}}\frac{\perm_{\pm}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\right)^{\prime}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\\
\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{\perm_{\pm}}\perm_{\pm}v^{(1)}_{\pm}+\left(\frac{\omega^{2}}{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\perm_{\pm}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{\perm_{\pm}}\perm_{\pm}v^{(2)}_{\pm}+\left(\frac{\omega^{2}}{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\perm_{\pm}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\frac{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\\
\sqrt{W_{\pm}}v^{(1)}_{\pm}-\frac{W_{\pm}^{\prime}}{2W_{\pm}\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}\frac{W_{\pm}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\sqrt{W_{\pm}}v^{(2)}_{\pm}-\frac{W_{\pm}^{\prime}}{2W_{\pm}\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}\frac{W_{\pm}}{|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\frac{\omega^{2}}{\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}\\
\sqrt{W_{\pm}}&-\frac{\omega^{2}W_{\pm}^{\prime}}{2W_{\pm}\sqrt{W_{\pm}}}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}v^{(1)}_{\pm}&v^{(2)}_{\pm}\\
\frac{W_{\pm}}{\omega^{2}\left(|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}\right)}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}&\frac{W_{\pm}}{\omega^{2}\left(|k|^{2}-W_{\pm}\right)}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}\end{array}\right)\ =\ {\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}\Phi_{\pm},

where
{\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}(x):=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\frac{\omega^{2}}{\sqrt{W_{\pm}(x)}}\\
\sqrt{W_{\pm}(x)}&-\frac{\omega^{2}W_{\pm}^{\prime}(x)}{2W_{\pm}(x)\sqrt{W_{\pm}(x)}}\end{array}\right)
.
Thus the fundamental matrix
\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}
of (
2.23
) satisfying
\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}(0)=I
is given by

\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{\pm}\widetilde{\Phi}_{\pm}(0)^{-1}={\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}\Phi_{\pm}\Phi_{\pm}(0)^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}(0)^{-1}={\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}\Phi_{\pm}T_{\pm}^{-1}.

Since
{\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}(a_{\pm})={\mathcal{T}}_{\pm}(0)=T_{\pm}
due to the
a_{\pm}
-periodicity of
\perm_{\pm}
, we have
\widehat{\Phi}_{\pm}(a_{\pm})=T_{\pm}\Phi_{\pm}(a_{\pm})T_{\pm}^{-1}
.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

The definition of
{\mathcal{S}}_{p}
includes the conditions
d(k),\tilde{d}(k)\neq 0
for all
k
. Together
with the asymptotic results in Lemmas
3.8
and
3.9
for
|k|\to\infty
, these conditions imply that
d
and
\widetilde{d}
are bounded away from
0
on the whole of
\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
.

Recall that the set
{\mathcal{S}}
is a subset of the resolvent set. Hence, the next result shows that
{\mathcal{S}}_{p}
lies in the resolvent set.

Proposition 3.7
.

We have
\mathcal{S}_{p}\subset\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

We need to check that for points
\omega\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
there are fundamental systems of (
2.15
) and (
2.16
) which satisfy the properties in Definition
3.1
. Note that (
3.2
) and (
3.4
) are satisfied by Remark
3.6
.
The proof is based on Lemmas
3.8
and
3.9
and subsequent estimates of the constants
\alpha^{\pm}_{j},j=1,\dots,6
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega
and assume that
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)
are
a_{\pm}
-periodic with
a_{\pm}>0
and satisfy

\perm_{+}(0,\omega)+\perm_{-}(0,\omega)\neq 0\quad\text{or}\quad\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0,\omega)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0,\omega)\neq 0.

(3.50)

If
D^{(v)}_{+}(k),D^{(v)}_{-}(k)\notin[-2,2]
for all
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
, then there exist
fundamental systems
\{v^{(1)}_{+},v^{(2)}_{+}\}
and
\{v^{(1)}_{-},v^{(2)}_{-}\}
of the homogeneous version of equation
(
2.15
) on
[0,\infty)
and on
(-\infty,0]
, respectively, such
that, for some (
k
-dependent) constants
\nu_{\pm}>0
,

C_{v,1,\pm}
,
C^{\prime}_{v,1,\pm}
,
C_{v,2,\pm}
,
C^{\prime}_{v,2,\pm}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|v^{(1)}_{\pm}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{v,1,\pm}\,e^{\nu_{\pm}x},

\displaystyle|v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{v,1,\pm}\,e^{\nu_{\pm}x},

\displaystyle|v^{(2)}_{\pm}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{v,2,\pm}\,e^{-\nu_{\pm}x},

\displaystyle|v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{v,2,\pm}\,e^{-\nu_{\pm}x}

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}_{\pm}

and

v^{(1)}_{+}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
v^{(2)}_{-}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
.
Consider
d
and
\tau_{\pm}
defined in (
3.2
) and (
3.5
) resp., and define

\delta:=\mbox{\rm dist}({\mathrm{Ran\,}}(\omega^{2}\perm(\cdot,\omega),\mathbb{R}_{+}).

(3.51)

(Note that
\delta>0
since
\omega\notin\Omega
.)

Then, as
|k|\to\infty
,

\displaystyle C_{v,1,\pm}

\displaystyle=C_{v,2,\pm}=\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1),

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{v,1,\pm}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}_{v,2,\pm}=\frac{|k|^{2}}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(|k|),

\displaystyle\tau_{\pm}

\displaystyle=2|k|+O(1),

\displaystyle\nu_{\pm}

\displaystyle=|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right),

and

\displaystyle d=\frac{\perm_{-}(0)+\perm_{+}(0)}{\sqrt{\perm_{+}(0)\,\perm_{-}(0)}}\,|k|+O(1);

if
\perm_{-}(0)+\perm_{+}(0)=0
, then we have the more precise asymptotics

d=\pm{\rm i}\,\frac{\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0)}{2\,\perm_{+}(0)}+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right).

Proof.

To shorten the notation we define again

W_{\pm}:=\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}.

We first focus on the interval
[0,\infty)
. Recall from Lemma
2.3
that equation (
2.15
) with
r=0
can be rewritten as (
2.23
), i.e.,
equation (
2.36
) for
z
with potential
V=W_{\pm}-\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{W_{\pm}^{\prime}}{W_{\pm}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{W_{\pm}^{\prime\prime}}{W_{\pm}}
and
l=|k|
.
We have Floquet solutions of
equation (
2.36
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
as in Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.6
. Due to the condition on the discriminant
D^{(v)}_{+}
, these solutions have the required exponential
behaviour with
\nu_{+}=\mathop{\rm Re}\kappa(|k|)>0
, see equation (
2.35
).

Now consider
|k|\geq k_{0}
, where
k_{0}
is the larger of the numbers
l_{0}
of
Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.6
applied to equation (
2.36
) on

\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Lemma
2.5
immediately gives the asymptotics
\nu_{+}=|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right).

The solution
z(x)=e^{\kappa(|k|)\,x}\,p_{1}(x)

(x\geq 0)
gives the solution
v^{(1)}_{+}
of equation (
2.15
):

v^{(1)}_{+}(x)=e^{\kappa(|k|)\,x}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{W_{+}}}\,\left(\kappa(|k|)\,p_{1}(x)+p_{1}^{\prime}(x)+\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}}{2W_{+}}\,p_{1}(x)\right)

with derivative

v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)=\frac{|k|^{2}-W_{+}}{\sqrt{W_{+}}}\,e^{\kappa(|k|)\,x}\,p_{1}(x).

Consequently we have by Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.6
for sufficiently large
|k|
, with
\nu_{+}=\mathop{\rm Re}\kappa(|k|)

\displaystyle|v^{(1)}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\nu_{+}x}\,\frac{1}{|\sqrt{W_{+}}|}\,\left(\left(|\kappa(|k|)|+\frac{1}{2}\,\left|\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}}{W_{+}}\right|\right)|p_{1}(x)|+|p_{1}^{\prime}(x)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\nu_{+}x}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}}\left(\left(|k|+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}}{W_{+}}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)

which gives the asserted asymptotics of
C_{v,1,+}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle|v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{||k|^{2}-W_{+}|}{|\sqrt{W_{+}}|}\,e^{\nu_{+}x}\,|p_{1}(x)|\leq e^{\nu_{+}x}\,\frac{|k|^{2}+\|W_{+}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{\delta}}\,\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)

for all
x\geq 0
, proving the asymptotics of
C^{\prime}_{v,1,+}
.

Next, the solution

z(x)=e^{-\kappa(|k|)\,x}\,p_{2}(x)

gives another solution
v^{(2)}_{+}
of equation (
2.15
) for
x\geq 0
:

\displaystyle v^{(2)}_{+}(x)

\displaystyle=e^{-\kappa(|k|)\,x}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{W_{+}}}\left(-\kappa(|k|)\,p_{2}(x)+p_{2}^{\prime}(x)+\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}}{2W_{+}}\,p_{2}(x)\right),

\displaystyle v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{|k|^{2}-W_{+}}{\sqrt{W_{+}}}\,e^{-\kappa(|k|)\,x}\,p_{2}(x).

Again we find for sufficiently large
|k|
and
x\geq 0
that

\displaystyle|v^{(2)}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\nu_{+}x}\,\frac{1}{|\sqrt{W_{+}}|}\left(\left(|\kappa(|k|)|+\frac{1}{2}\left|\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}}{W_{+}}\right|\right)|p_{2}(x)|+|p_{2}^{\prime}(x)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\nu_{+}x}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}}\,\left(\left(|k|+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}+\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}}{W_{+}}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)

and

|v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)|\leq e^{-\nu_{+}x}\,\frac{|k|^{2}+\|W_{+}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{\delta}}\,\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right).

These last two inequalities prove the asserted asymptotics of
C_{v,2,+}
and
C^{\prime}_{v,2,+}
.
The Wronski determinant is

\displaystyle\tau_{+}

\displaystyle=(v^{(1)}_{+}\,v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}-v_{+}^{(1)^{\prime}}\,v^{(2)}_{+})(0)\,\frac{W_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\left(\kappa(|k|)\,p_{1}(0)+p_{1}^{\prime}(0)+\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}(0)}{2W_{+}(0)}\,p_{1}(0)\right)\frac{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,p_{2}(0)\,\frac{W_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,p_{1}(0)\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\left(-\kappa(|k|)\,p_{2}(0)+p_{2}^{\prime}(0)+\frac{W^{\prime}_{+}(0)}{2W_{+}(0)}\,p_{2}(0)\right)\frac{W_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}

\displaystyle=2\kappa(|k|)\,p_{1}(0)\,p_{2}(0)+p_{1}^{\prime}(0)\,p_{2}(0)-p_{1}(0)\,p_{2}^{\prime}(0)

\displaystyle=2|k|\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\right)\right)\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)^{2}+2\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)

\displaystyle=2|k|+O(1).

Analogous estimates apply to
v^{(1)}_{-}
,
v^{(2)}_{-}
,
v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}
,
v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{-}
, and
\tau_{-}
but
with different
\kappa(|k|)
(which has real part
\nu_{-}
) and
W_{-}
instead of

W_{+}
, thus proving the asserted asymptotics for
C_{v,1,-}
,
C^{\prime}_{v,1,-}
,
C_{v,2,-}
and
C^{\prime}_{v,2,-}
.
Finally, denoting by
\widehat{p}_{1}
,
\widehat{p}_{2}
the periodic functions which arise
on
(-\infty,0]
as counterparts of
p_{1},p_{2}
on
[0,\infty)
, we find

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle=-v^{(2)}_{+}(0)\,v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)\,\frac{W_{-}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{-}(0)}+v^{(1)}_{-}(0)\,v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)\,\frac{W_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\left(-\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,p_{2}(0)+p_{2}^{\prime}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{W^{\prime}_{+}(0)}{W_{+}(0)}\,p_{2}(0)\right)\frac{|k|^{2}-W_{-}(0)}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)\,\frac{W_{-}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{-}(0)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\left(\kappa_{-}(|k|)\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)+\widehat{p}_{1}^{\prime}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{W^{\prime}_{-}(0)}{W_{-}(0)}\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)\right)\frac{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,p_{2}(0)\,\frac{W_{+}(0)}{|k|^{2}-W_{+}(0)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,\kappa_{+}(|k|)+\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,\kappa_{-}(|k|)\right)\widehat{p}_{1}(0)\,p_{2}(0)-\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,p_{2}^{\prime}(0)\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)+\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,\widehat{p}_{1}^{\prime}(0)\,p_{2}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{W^{\prime}_{+}(0)}{W_{+}(0)}\,p_{2}(0)\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)+\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{W^{\prime}_{-}(0)}{W_{-}(0)}\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)\,p_{2}(0).

If
W_{+}(0)+W_{-}(0)\neq 0
, we hence obtain

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,|k|\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\right)\right)+\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,|k|\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\right)\right)\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)+\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{W^{\prime}_{+}(0)}{W_{+}(0)}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{W^{\prime}_{-}(0)}{W_{-}(0)}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{W_{-}(0)+W_{+}(0)}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)\,W_{-}(0)}}\,|k|+O(1).

In the case
W_{+}(0)+W_{-}(0)=0
, we have

\frac{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}=-\frac{\sqrt{W_{+}(0)}}{\sqrt{W_{-}(0)}}\in\{{\rm i},-{\rm i}\},

so

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle=\pm{\rm i}\Bigg((\kappa_{+}(|k|)-\kappa_{-}(|k|))\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)\,p_{2}(0)-p_{2}^{\prime}(0)\,\widehat{p}_{1}(0)-\widehat{p}_{1}^{\prime}(0)\,p_{2}(0)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}(0)}{W_{+}(0)}+\frac{W_{-}^{\prime}(0)}{W_{-}(0)}\right)p_{2}(0)\widehat{p}_{1}(0)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mp{\rm i}\,\frac{W_{+}^{\prime}(0)-W_{-}^{\prime}(0)}{2\,W_{+}(0)}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right).

∎

Lemma
3.8
shows that
\omega\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}
satisfies (i) and the estimates of the fundamental system in (ii) of Definition
3.1
. Next, we prove that also for equation (
2.16
) there is a fundamental system satisfying the corresponding properties of Definition
3.1
. The proof is less technical than for the
v
-component treated by Lemma
3.8
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega
and assume (
1.3
).
If the discriminants
D^{(w)}_{+}(k),D^{(w)}_{-}(k)\notin[-2,2]
for all
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
, then
there exist fundamental systems
\{w^{(1)}_{+},w^{(2)}_{+}\}
and

\{w^{(1)}_{-},w^{(2)}_{-}\}
of equation (
2.16
) on

[0,\infty)
and on
(-\infty,0]
, respectively such that, for some
(
k
-dependent) constants

\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}>0
,
C_{w,1,\pm},C^{\prime}_{w,1,\pm},C_{w,2,\pm},C^{\prime}_{w,2,\pm}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|w^{(1)}_{\pm}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{w,1,\pm}\,e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}x},\qquad|w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{\pm}(x)|\leq C^{\prime}_{w,1,\pm}\,e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}x},

\displaystyle|w^{(2)}_{\pm}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{w,2,\pm}\,e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}x},\qquad|w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{\pm}(x)|\leq C^{\prime}_{w,2,\pm}\,e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}x}

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}_{\pm}

and

w^{(1)}_{+}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
w^{(2)}_{-}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
.
Consider
\widetilde{d}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{\pm}
defined in (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) resp. Then, as
k\to\infty
,

\displaystyle C_{w,1,\pm}

\displaystyle=C_{w,2,\pm}=1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right),

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{w,1,\pm}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}_{w,2,\pm}=|k|+O(1),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=-2|k|+O(1),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right),

and

\widetilde{d}=-2|k|+O(1).

Proof.

Just like in Lemma
3.8
we set
\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}=\mathop{\rm Re}\kappa_{\pm}(|k|)>0
, see equation (
2.35
). Note that equation (
2.16
) has the form of (
2.36
) with
V=\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
and
l=|k|^{2}
. The asymptotics of
\widetilde{\nu}_{\pm}
follow immediately from Lemma
2.5
.

Next, we focus on the interval
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We have, with periodic
functions
p_{1},p_{2}
as in Lemma
2.6
,

w^{(1)}_{+}(x)=e^{\kappa_{+}(|k|)x}\,p_{1}(x),\quad w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)=(\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,p_{1}(x)+p_{1}^{\prime}(x))\,e^{\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,x}\quad(x\geq 0),

and hence

\displaystyle|w^{(1)}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle=e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,|p_{1}(x)|\leq e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,(1+|p_{1}(x)-1|)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right),

\displaystyle|w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,(|\kappa_{+}(|k|)|\,|p_{1}(x)|+|p_{1}^{\prime}(x)|)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,\left(\left(|k|+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)\,\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)\leq e^{\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,(|k|+\mathord{\rm const})

for all
x\leq 0
by Lemma
2.5
and
2.6
.
Similarly,

w^{(2)}_{+}(x)=e^{-\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,x}\,p_{2}(x),\quad w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)=(-\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,p_{2}(x)+p_{2}^{\prime}(x))\,e^{-\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,x}\quad(x\geq 0),

so

\displaystyle|w^{(2)}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle=e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,|p_{2}(x)|\leq e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right),

\displaystyle|w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,(|\kappa_{+}(|k|)|\,|p_{2}(x)|+|p_{2}^{\prime}(x)|)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,\left(\left(|k|+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)\,\left(1+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)+\frac{\mathord{\rm const}}{|k|}\right)\leq e^{-\widetilde{\nu}_{+}x}\,(|k|+\mathord{\rm const})

for all
x\geq 0
. These estimates prove the asymptotics of
C_{w,1,+}
,
C^{\prime}_{w,1,+}
,
C_{w,2,+}
and
C^{\prime}_{w,2,+}
.
For the Wronski determinant we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{+}

\displaystyle=w^{(1)}_{+}(0)w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)-w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)w^{(2)}_{+}(0)

\displaystyle=-2\kappa_{+}(|k|)\,p_{1}(0)\,p_{2}(0)+p_{1}(0)\,p_{2}^{\prime}(0)-p_{1}^{\prime}(0)\,p_{2}(0)

\displaystyle=-2|k|\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\right)\right)\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)^{2}+2\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)

\displaystyle=-2|k|+O(1).

Analogous estimates apply to
w^{(1)}_{-}
,
w^{(2)}_{-}
,
w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}
,
w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{-}
, and
\widetilde{\tau}_{-}
but with
\widetilde{\nu}_{-}=\mathop{\rm Re}\kappa_{-}(|k|)
.
Finally,

\displaystyle\widetilde{d}

\displaystyle=-w^{(2)}_{+}(0)\,w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0)+w^{(1)}_{-}(0)\,w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0)

\displaystyle=-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,\left(|k|\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\right)\right)\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(-|k|\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\right)\right)\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-2|k|+O(1).

∎

To finish the proof of Proposition
3.7
, it remains to provide (in 1-6 below)
k
-independent bounds of
\alpha^{\pm}_{j},j=1,\dots,6
from Definition
3.1
. All the asymptotic expansions below are for
|k|\to\infty
. We use
\delta
as defined in (
3.51
).

1)

\alpha_{1}^{\pm}=\frac{(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1))^{2}}{(2|k|+O(1))\,(1+|k|^{2})\,(|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right))}=\frac{1}{2\delta\,|k|^{2}}\,(1+o(1)).

2)
If
\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1)}{\left(\left|\frac{\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)}{\sqrt{\perm_{+}(0)\perm_{-}(0)}}\right|\,|k|+O(1)\right)\,(1+|k|^{2})\,\sqrt{|k|}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)}\times

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\left(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1)\right)\,\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{|k|}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)}\right)\right.+\left.\frac{2(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1))^{2}\,(\frac{|k|^{2}}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(|k|))}{(2|k|+O(1))\,(1+|k|^{2})\,\sqrt{|k|}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{|\sqrt{\perm_{+}(0)\perm_{-}(0)}|}{\sqrt{\delta}\,|\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)|}\,|k|^{-5/2}\,(1+o(1))\left(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+o(|k|)+\frac{\sqrt{|k|}}{\sqrt{\delta}^{3}}\,(1+o(1))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{|\sqrt{\perm_{+}(0)\perm_{-}(0)}|}{\delta\,|\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)|}\,|k|^{-3/2}\,(1+o(1)).

If
\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)=0
and
\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0)\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{+}=\frac{2\,|\perm_{+}(0)|}{\delta\,|\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0)|}\,|k|^{-1/2}\,(1+o(1)).

3)
If
\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+|k|^{3})\,\sqrt{|k|\,(1+O(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}))}}\left(\frac{\frac{|k|^{2}}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(|k|)}{\left|\frac{\perm_{-}(0)+\perm_{+}(0)}{\sqrt{\perm_{-}(0)\perm_{+}(0)}}\right|\,|k|+O(1)}\left(\left(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1)\right)\,\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{|k|\,(1+O(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}))}}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\frac{2\,(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1))^{2}\,(\frac{|k|^{2}}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(|k|))}{(2|k|+O(1))\,(1+|k|^{2})\,\sqrt{|k|\,(1+O(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}))}}\right)+\left.\frac{2(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(1))\,(\frac{|k|^{2}}{\sqrt{\delta}}+O(|k|))}{(2|k|+O(1))\,\sqrt{|k|\,(1+O(\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}))}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{|k|^{7/2}}\left(\frac{|k|+O(1)}{\sqrt{\delta}\left|\frac{\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)}{\sqrt{\perm_{+}(0)\perm_{-}(0)}}\right|}\left(\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\delta}}\,(1+o(1))+\frac{\sqrt{|k|}\,(1+o(1))}{\sqrt{\delta}^{3}}\right)+\frac{|k|^{3/2}}{\delta}\,(1+o(1))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{|\sqrt{\perm_{+}(0)\perm_{-}(0)}|}{\delta\,|\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)|}\,|k|^{-3/2}\,(1+o(1)).

If
\perm_{+}(0)+\perm_{-}(0)=0
and
\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0)\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\,|\perm_{+}(0)|}{\delta\,|\perm_{+}^{\prime}(0)-\perm_{-}^{\prime}(0)|}\,|k|^{-1/2}\,(1+o(1)).

4)

\alpha_{4}^{\pm}=\frac{\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)^{2}}{(2|k|+O(1))\,\left(|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)}\,(1+|k|)=\frac{1}{2|k|}\,(1+o(1)).

5)

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,(1+|k|)}{(2|k|+O(1))\sqrt{|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}}\left(2\,\frac{\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,(|k|+O(1))}{(2|k|+O(1))\sqrt{|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)+\frac{1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}{\sqrt{|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{2\sqrt{|k|}}\left(\frac{1+o(1)}{\sqrt{|k|}}+\frac{1+o(1)}{\sqrt{|k|}}\right)=\frac{1}{|k|}\,(1+o(1)).

6)

\displaystyle\alpha_{6}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{|k|+O(1)}{(2|k|+O(1))\sqrt{|k|+O(\frac{1}{|k|})}}\left(\frac{2\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,(|k|+O(1))}{(2|k|+O(1))\,\sqrt{|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}}\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)+\frac{1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}{\sqrt{|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\,\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)\,(|k|+O(1))}{(2|k|+O(1))\,\left(|k|+O\left(\frac{1}{|k|}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{2\sqrt{|k|}}\left(\frac{1+o(1)}{\sqrt{|k|}}+\frac{1+o(1)}{\sqrt{|k|}}\right)+\frac{1+o(1)}{|k|}=\frac{2}{|k|}\,(1+o(1)).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
3.7
.
∎

4
Weyl Spectrum

We study two sources of the Weyl spectrum, namely radiation orthogonal to and along the interface.
In Sec.
4.1
we construct Weyl sequences with support moving to infinity in the
x_{1}
-direction. These correspond physically to radiation in Maxwell’s equations in the
x_{1}
-direction, i.e., orthogonal to the interface. In Sec.
4.2
we construct Weyl sequences the support of which is localized near the interface
x_{1}=0
and moves to infinity in the
x_{\parallel}
-variables. Recall that
x_{\parallel}=x_{2}
if
N=2
and
x_{\parallel}=(x_{2},x_{3})^{\top}
if
N=3
. Physically, these sequences describe radiation along the interface (guided modes).

4.1
Radiation orthogonal to the interface

We first consider the case of homogeneous media in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}
to compare the results with those in
[
7
]
and because the analysis is considerably simpler than in the case of a general
x_{1}
dependence of
\perm_{\pm}
. Later, in Sec.
4.1.2
, we study the case of general
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)
. Finally, in Sec.
4.1.3
we apply the general results to the case when
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)
are periodic or asymptotically periodic.

4.1.1
Media Homogeneous in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}

Let us consider the case
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)=\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
separately. Just as in
[
7
]
for the one and two dimensional cases, we show that for any
\omega\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\pm}^{\mathrm{red}}
, defined in (
3.40
), a Weyl sequence traveling to
x_{1}\to\pm\infty
can be constructed.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)=\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
. Then

{\mathcal{M}}_{+}^{\mathrm{red}}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{-}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
.

Proof.

We study
\omega\in{\mathcal{M}}_{+}^{\mathrm{red}}
. The case
\omega\in{\mathcal{M}}_{-}^{\mathrm{red}}
can be treated in a completely analogous manner.

Recall that a Weyl sequence at
\omega\in D(\perm)
is a sequence
(E^{(n)})_{n}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
such that
\|E^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=1\ \forall n
,
E^{(n)}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
and
\|L(\omega)E^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. It is easy to see that one can equivalently check these properties in the Fourier variables. Let
u^{(n)}:=\widehat{E}^{(n)}
, where
\penalty\ \widehat{\ }\penalty\
is the Fourier transform with respect to the variables
x_{\parallel}
. Due to the Plancherel identity one has
\|u^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=\|E^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}
and
\|L(\omega)E^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=\|\widehat{L}(\omega)u^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}
. Moreover, using the Parseval identity, we have
(E^{(n)},\psi)_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=(u^{(n)},\widehat{\psi})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}
for all
\psi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
. Because
\ \widehat{\ }:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
is an isometric isomorphism, we conclude that
E^{(n)}\rightharpoonup 0
if and only if
u^{(n)}\rightharpoonup 0
.

With the help of Lemma
2.2
we can also work in the transformed variables
\xi:=(u_{1},v,w)^{\top}
instead of
u
and with the transformed operator
\widetilde{L}
, defined in (
2.4
), instead of
\widehat{L}
.
Applying the above argument again, we can also study the inverse Fourier transform of
\xi
, denoted by
\widecheck{\xi}
. Hence, below we find a sequence
(\xi^{(n)})\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
with
\xi^{(n)}=(u_{1}^{(n)},v^{(n)},w^{(n)})^{\top}
such that
\|\xi^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=1
,
\widecheck{\xi}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
and
\|(\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi^{(n)})\widecheck{\phantom{a}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

Again, we use the notation
k=k_{2}
if
N=2
and
k=(k_{2},k_{3})^{\top}
if
N=3
. Let
k_{0}=k_{0,2}
if
N=2
and
k_{0}=(k_{0,2},k_{0,3})^{\top}
if
N=3
be such that
|k_{0}|^{2}<\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)
. Recall that
\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)>0
due to
\omega\in{\mathcal{M}}_{+}^{\mathrm{red}}
. We set
\xi^{(n)}=(u_{1}^{(n)},v^{(n)},w^{(n)})^{\top}
with

w^{(n)}(x_{1},k):=n^{\frac{N}{2}-1}\widehat{\varphi}\left(\frac{x_{1}-n^{2}}{n},n(k-k_{0})\right)e^{{\rm i}\mu x_{1}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\ u_{1}^{(n)}=v^{(n)}=0,

(4.1)

where

\mu=\sqrt{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)-|k_{0}|^{2}}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}

and
\widehat{\varphi}
is the Fourier transform (with respect to
x_{\parallel}
) of a scalar-valued function
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
with
\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}=1
.
Note that
e^{{\rm i}\mu x_{1}}
solves the homogeneous
w-
equation (
2.16
) (with
k=k_{0}
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and that
w^{(n)}
is the Fourier-transform in the
x_{\parallel}
-variables of the truncated plane wave

\widecheck{w}^{(n)}(x)=n^{-\frac{N}{2}}e^{{\rm i}\left(k_{0}\cdot x_{\parallel}+\mu x_{1}\right)}\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-n^{2}}{n},\frac{x_{\parallel}}{n}\right).

(4.2)

Since
\varphi
is compactly supported, for sufficiently large
n
the function
\xi^{(n)}
is supported away from the interface
x_{1}=0
. Hence, it trivially satisfies the interface conditions in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
. Due to the normalization of
\varphi
we have
\|\xi^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=1
.
The divergence condition
(u_{1}^{(n)})^{\prime}=-{\rm i}|k_{0}|v^{(n)}
holds also trivially as
u_{1}^{(n)}=v^{(n)}=0
.
Finally, the
L^{2}
-conditions in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
are satisfied due to
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. We conclude that
\xi^{(n)}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
for all
n
.

It remains to check that
(\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi^{(n)})\widecheck{\phantom{a}}\to 0
and that
\widecheck{\xi}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup 0
. As

\displaystyle(\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi^{(n)})\widecheck{\phantom{a}}=

\displaystyle\penalty\displaystyle(-\Delta\widecheck{w}^{(n)}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\widecheck{w}^{(n)})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\displaystyle-e^{{\rm i}\left(k_{0}\cdot x_{\parallel}+\mu x_{1}\right)}\left[n^{-\frac{N}{2}-2}\Delta\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-n^{2}}{n},\frac{x_{\parallel}}{n}\right)+2{\rm i}n^{-\frac{N}{2}-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mu\\
k_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot\nabla\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-n^{2}}{n},\frac{x_{\parallel}}{n}\right)\right]\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right),

we have

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq c\left(n^{-2}\|\Delta\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}+n^{-1}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\right)\to 0\ (n\to\infty).

Finally, to show
\widecheck{\xi}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup 0
, it suffices to check that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\eta(x)\widecheck{w}^{(n)}(x)\,{\rm d}x\to 0\ \forall\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N},\mathbb{R})
. For
n
large enough we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\eta(x)\widecheck{w}^{(n)}(x)\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{N}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\left|\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-n^{2}}{n},\frac{x_{\parallel}}{n}\right)\eta(x_{1},x_{\parallel})\right|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{N}{2}}\int_{[n,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}\left|\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-n^{2}}{n},\frac{x_{\parallel}}{n}\right)\eta(x_{1},x_{\parallel})\right|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}((n,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0.

∎

Remark 4.2
.

In the proof of Lemma
4.1
we construct Weyl sequences only of the simple form
(0,0,w_{n})^{\top}
.
Also Weyl sequences of the form
(u_{1,n},v_{n},0)^{\top}
are conceivable but in view of the fact that Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.15
and Lemma
5.2
describe the whole spectrum, as explained below Theorem
B
, such sequences will generate no additional spectrum outside
\Omega_{0}
.

4.1.2
General
\perm

With
\Omega^{\pm}_{a}
for
a>0
introduced in (
1.6
), we define

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{e,v}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\omega\in D(\perm):\text{ there is }\ k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\ \text{such that}\ \omega\notin\Omega^{\pm}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}\text{ and there exists}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\text{a solution}\ v\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})\ \text{of}\ \eqref{3}\ \text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{\pm}\ \text{with}\ k=k_{0}\ \text{and}\ r=0\ \text{such that}\ \eqref{E:seq-supp}\ \text{holds}\right\}

and

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{e,w}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\omega\in D(\perm):\text{ there is }\ k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\ \text{such that}\ \text{there exists}\ \text{a solution}\ w\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})\ \text{of}\ \eqref{4}\ \text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{\pm}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\ \text{with}\ k=k_{0}\ \text{and}\ r=0\ \text{such that}\ \eqref{E:seq-supp}\ \text{holds with}\ w\ \text{instead of}\ v\right\},

where condition (
4.3
) reads

\displaystyle\exists\penalty\ \varepsilon,c>0,(R_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}_{\pm},(l_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}:|R_{n}|,l_{n}\to\infty,\ l_{n}\leq\frac{|R_{n}|}{2}\ \text{and}

(4.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2l_{n}}\text{meas}\{x\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|v(x)|\geq\varepsilon\}\geq c\ \forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

Remark 4.3
.

The condition
\omega\notin\Omega^{\pm}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}
, i.e.,
\mbox{\rm dist}(\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm},\omega),{|k_{0}|^{2}})>0
, is not needed in
\Sigma_{e,w}^{\pm}
because equation (
2.16
) does not contain
|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(x_{1},\omega)
as a denominator.

Remark 4.4
.

Condition (
4.3
) means that one can find a sequence of intervals moving to infinity with their lengths also diverging to infinity and such that the solution
v
is bounded away from zero on a substantial part of these intervals.

Note that (
4.3
) (in the “
+
case”) is equivalent to the existence of
\varepsilon>0
such that

\limsup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R,l\to\infty}}{{l\leq R/2}}}\frac{1}{2l}{\rm meas}\{x\in[R-l,R+l]:|v(x)|\geq\varepsilon\}>0.

Remark 4.5
.

Condition (
4.3
) is generally difficult to check. However, as we show in Section
4.1.3
, see Lemma
4.7
, it can be easily satisfied in the case of periodic media.

Theorem 4.6
.

\Sigma_{e,v}^{+}\cup\Sigma_{e,v}^{-}\cup\Sigma_{e,w}^{+}\cup\Sigma_{e,w}^{-}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})

Proof.

We prove
\Sigma_{e,v}^{+}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
in detail and comment on the simpler case
\Sigma_{e,w}^{+}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
at the end. The remaining two statements are proved analogously.

Let
\omega\in\Sigma_{e,v}^{+}
and
k_{0}
and
v
be like in the definition of
\Sigma_{e,v}^{+}
. To construct a Weyl sequence, we first define

v_{n}(x_{1},k_{2},k_{3}):=\frac{n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}}{\sqrt{l_{n}}}\zeta(n(k-k_{0}))\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v(x_{1}),

(4.4)

where
\zeta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1},\mathbb{R})
is arbitrary and
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})
satisfies

\mbox{\rm supp}\penalty\ \varphi\subset[-1,\infty),\quad\varphi>0\quad\text{on}\ (-1,1].

(4.5)

Next, we calculate

\displaystyle\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{N-1}}{l_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\zeta(n(k-k_{0}))^{2}\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)^{2}|v(x_{1})|^{2}\,{\rm d}(x_{1},k)

(4.6)

\displaystyle=\|\zeta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(y_{1})^{2}|v(R_{n}+l_{n}y_{1})|^{2}\,{\rm d}y_{1}.

Since
v
is bounded, (
4.6
) shows that
\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}
is bounded from above independently of
n
. Furthermore, denoting

M_{n}:=\{y_{1}\in[-1,1]:|v(R_{n}+l_{n}y_{1})|\geq\varepsilon\}

and using (
4.3
), we get

\text{meas}\penalty\ M_{n}=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\text{meas}\penalty\ \{x_{1}\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|v(x_{1})|\geq\varepsilon\}\geq\widetilde{c},

where
\widetilde{c}:=\min\{2c,3\}
is introduced for a technical reason explained next. Because
\text{meas}\penalty\ (M_{n}\cap[-1,-1+\tfrac{\widetilde{c}}{2}])\leq\tfrac{\widetilde{c}}{2}
, we therefore get

\text{meas}\penalty\ \left(M_{n}\cap\left[-1+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{2},1\right]\right)\geq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{2}.

Note that the interval
\left[-1+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{2},1\right]
is non-empty because
\widetilde{c}\leq 3.

Using this estimate, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(y_{1})^{2}|v(R_{n}+l_{n}y_{1})|^{2}\,{\rm d}y_{1}\geq\varepsilon^{2}\int_{M_{n}}\varphi(y_{1})^{2}\,{\rm d}y_{1}\geq\varepsilon^{2}\int_{M_{n}\cap[-1+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{2},1]}\varphi(y_{1})^{2}\,{\rm d}y_{1}\geq\varepsilon^{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{2}\min_{[-1+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{2},1]}\varphi^{2}>0.

Hence, (
4.6
) implies that
\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}
is bounded also from below independently of
n
.

For the rest of the proof, once again, we use the notation

W:=W(x_{1}):=\omega^{2}\perm(x_{1},\omega).

Motivated by (
2.17
) (with
r=0
), we define the corresponding component
u_{1}
by

u_{1,n}(x_{1},k):=\frac{-{\rm i}|k_{0}|v_{n}^{\prime}(x_{1},k)}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W(x_{1})}.

Since the resulting vectors
(u_{1,n},v_{n},0)
in general do not satisfy the divergence condition, we modify the second component to obtain our proposed Weyl sequence

U_{n}:=\begin{pmatrix}u_{1,n}\\
v_{n}+r_{n}\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{where}\quad r_{n}(x_{1},k):=\frac{1}{W(x_{1})}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}\left(\frac{W(x_{1})v_{n}^{\prime}(x_{1},k)}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W(x_{1})}\right)^{\prime}-v_{n}(x_{1},k).

(4.7)

To show that
U_{n}
is a Weyl sequence, we start by proving
U_{n}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\widetilde{L}U_{n}\to 0
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
, where
\widetilde{L}
was defined in (
2.4
). First note that besides
v_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
(shown above), one also has
v_{n}^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
and by (
2.15
) also
v_{n}^{\prime\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. It follows that
u_{1,n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. Next, we show that
r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime},r_{n}^{\prime\prime}\to 0
in
L^{2}
. Abbreviating

\rho_{n,k}:=\frac{n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}}{\sqrt{l_{n}}}\zeta(n(k-k_{0})),

we obtain

\displaystyle r_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho_{n,k}}{W}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}\left[\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\left(\varphi\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v^{\prime}+\frac{1}{l_{n}}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v\right)\right]^{\prime}-v_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}-1\right)v_{n}+\frac{\rho_{n,k}}{W}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}\left\{\frac{2}{l_{n}}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}v^{\prime}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\frac{1}{l_{n}}\left[\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)+\frac{1}{l_{n}}\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\varphi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)\right]v\right\}

\displaystyle=:\left(\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}-1\right)v_{n}+\frac{\rho_{n,k}}{W}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}A_{n}(v,\varphi),

where we have used the differential equation for
v
, i.e.,
\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}v^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}=Wv
. Next, differentiating
r_{n}
yields

\displaystyle r_{n}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}-1\right)v_{n}^{\prime}-\frac{\rho_{n,k}W^{\prime}}{W^{2}}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}A_{n}(v,\varphi)

(4.8)

\displaystyle+\frac{\rho_{n,k}}{W}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|l_{n}}\left\{2\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)Wv+\left[\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)+\frac{3}{l_{n}}\varphi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right]v^{\prime}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime\prime}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)+\frac{2}{l_{n}}\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)+\frac{1}{l_{n}^{2}}\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}\left(\frac{\cdot-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)\right]v\right\}.

Differentiating once more, we get
r_{n}^{\prime\prime}
in terms of
v_{n}^{\prime\prime},v,v^{\prime},
and
v^{\prime\prime}
. The formula for
r_{n}^{\prime\prime}
includes
W^{\prime\prime\prime}
. This is where the assumption
\perm_{\pm}\in W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
from (
1.3
) is fully used. Because
v\in C^{1}_{b}
and with the use of the differential equation (
2.15
), we get that
v,v^{\prime},v^{\prime\prime}
are bounded on
[0,\infty)
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}\rho_{n,k}^{2}\left(\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}-1\right)^{2}\,{\rm d}k=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}\zeta(\kappa)^{2}\left(\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k_{0}+\tfrac{1}{n}\kappa|}-1\right)^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa\to 0\ (n\to\infty)

(4.9)

due to the compact support of
\zeta
. Similarly, one shows that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}\rho_{n,k}^{2}\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}}{|k|^{2}}\,{\rm d}k\leq\frac{c}{l_{n}}

(4.10)

for all
n
large enough with
c
independent of
n
. Thanks to (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) and using the formulas for
r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime}
and
r_{n}^{\prime\prime}
, we have
r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime},r_{n}^{\prime\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
and

r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime},r_{n}^{\prime\prime}\to 0\quad\text{in}\ L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}).

(4.11)

In particular, we have now shown that
U_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
.
From (
4.8
) we can derive in a similar way that
|k|r_{n}^{\prime}\to 0
in
L^{2}
, and therefore

\widetilde{L}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
r_{n}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\to 0\quad\text{in}\ L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}).

(4.12)

Straightforward estimates using the compact support of
\varphi
and
\zeta
and the boundednness of
v
,
v^{\prime}
, and
v^{\prime\prime}
produce the remaining

L^{2}
-properties in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
, i.e.,

(v_{n}+r_{n})^{\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u_{1,n},\ (v_{n}+r_{n})^{\prime\prime}-{\rm i}|k|u_{1,n}^{\prime},\ {\rm i}|k|(v_{n}+r_{n})^{\prime}+|k|^{2}u_{1,n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}).

The interface conditions in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
, i.e.,

\llbracket\perm u_{1,n}\rrbracket=\llbracket v_{n}+r_{n}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\rm i}|k|u_{1,n}-(v_{n}+r_{n})^{\prime}\rrbracket=0

are trivially satisfied because due to (
4.5
) we have
v_{n}(x_{1})=0
for
x_{1}\leq R_{n}/2
, and all terms in
u_{1,n}
and
r_{n}
are proportional either to
v_{n}
or
v_{n}^{\prime}
.

The last property needed to conclude
U_{n}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
is the divergence condition. This holds automatically since

(Wu_{1,n})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k|W(v_{n}+r_{n})=(Wu_{1,n})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k_{0}|\left(\frac{Wv_{n}^{\prime}}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}=0

using the definition of
u_{1,n}
and
r_{n}
.

Next, we need to show that
\widetilde{L}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{1,n}\\
v_{n}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\to 0
in
L^{2}
. We calculate

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{1,n}\\
v_{n}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}(|k|^{2}-W)u_{1,n}+{\rm i}|k|v_{n}^{\prime}\\
{\rm i}|k|u_{1,n}^{\prime}-v_{n}^{\prime\prime}-Wv_{n}\\
0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}(|k_{0}|^{2}-W)u_{1,n}+{\rm i}|k_{0}|v_{n}^{\prime}\\
{\rm i}|k_{0}|u_{1,n}^{\prime}-v_{n}^{\prime\prime}-Wv_{n}\\
0\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})u_{1,n}+{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)v_{n}^{\prime}\\
{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)u_{1,n}^{\prime}\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}v_{n}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}-Wv_{n}\\
0\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})u_{1,n}+{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)v_{n}^{\prime}\\
{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)u_{1,n}^{\prime}\\
0\end{pmatrix},

(4.13)

where we have used the definition of
u_{1,n}
and the identity
\left(\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}v_{n}^{\prime}}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}-v_{n}^{\prime\prime}=\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}v_{n}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}
.

We denote the second component of the first term in (
4.13
) by
B_{n}
, i.e.,
B_{n}:=\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}v_{n}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}-Wv_{n}
and obtain

\displaystyle B_{n}(x_{1},k)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}}{\sqrt{l_{n}}}\zeta(n(k-k_{0}))\left[\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\left(\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v\right)^{\prime\prime}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\left(\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}\left(\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v\right)^{\prime}-W\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}}{\sqrt{l_{n}}}\zeta(n(k-k_{0}))\left\{\frac{1}{l_{n}^{2}}\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\varphi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\frac{1}{l_{n}}\left[2\frac{W}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v^{\prime}+\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}W^{\prime}}{(|k_{0}|^{2}-W)^{2}}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)v\right]\right\}.

As
v\in C^{1}_{b}([0,\infty))
and
\mbox{\rm dist}(W(\mathbb{R}),\{|k_{0}|^{2}\})>0
, we get

|B_{n}(x_{1},k)|\leq C\frac{n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}}{\sqrt{l_{n}}}|\zeta(n(k-k_{0}))|\left(\frac{1}{l_{n}^{2}}\left|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)\right|+\frac{1}{l_{n}}\left|\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)\right|\right)

leading to

\|B_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{2}\leq 2C^{2}\|\zeta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{l_{n}^{4}}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\frac{1}{l_{n}^{2}}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{c}{l_{n}^{2}}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty).

In order to estimate the second vector in (
4.13
), i.e.,

C_{n}(x_{1},k):=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})u_{1,n}+{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)v_{n}^{\prime}\\
{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)u_{1,n}^{\prime}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right),

first note that
||k|-|k_{0}||\leq|k-k_{0}|
and
||k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2}|\leq|k-k_{0}|(|k|+|k_{0}|)
. Hence,

\displaystyle|C_{n}(x_{1},k)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\begin{pmatrix}-{\rm i}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})\frac{|k_{0}|v_{n}^{\prime}}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}+{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)v_{n}^{\prime}\\
(|k|-|k_{0}|)\left(\frac{|k_{0}|v_{n}^{\prime}}{|k_{0}|^{2}-W}\right)^{\prime}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\right|^{2}\leq C|k-k_{0}|^{2}\left[(|k|+|k_{0}|)^{2}|v_{n}^{\prime}|^{2}+|v_{n}^{\prime}|^{2}+|v_{n}^{\prime\prime}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C|k-k_{0}|^{2}\frac{n^{N-1}}{l_{n}}\zeta(n(k-k_{0}))^{2}(|k|^{2}+1)\left[\varphi\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)^{2}+\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)^{2}+\varphi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x_{1}-R_{n}}{l_{n}}\right)^{2}\right],

where we have used the boundedness of
v,v^{\prime},
and
v^{\prime\prime}
on
\mathbb{R}
. The fact that the second derivative
v^{\prime\prime}
is bounded follows from the boundedness of
v
and
v^{\prime}
and from the differential equation (
2.15
) (with
r=0
).

For the
L^{2}-
norm we obtain

\displaystyle\|C_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}\zeta(\kappa)^{2}\frac{|\kappa|^{2}}{n^{2}}\left[\left|k_{0}+\frac{\kappa}{n}\right|^{2}+1\right]\,{\rm d}\kappa\left(\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-2}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty),

leading to the conclusion
\widetilde{L}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{1,n}\\
v_{n}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\to 0
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
and together with (
4.12
) we get
\widetilde{L}U_{n}\to 0
in
L^{2}
.

It remains to be proved that
U_{n}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
. As shown in (
4.6
),
\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}}
is bounded above independently of
n
. Differentiating
v_{n}
and using that
v^{\prime}
is bounded by assumption, we find similarly to (
4.6
) that also
\|v_{n}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}
is bounded above independently of
n
. By the definition of
u_{1,n}
and the convergence in (
4.11
), we find that

\|U_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq C\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}

(4.14)

with
C>0
independent of
n
. For any
\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
we find

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}U_{n}\cdot\eta\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{R_{n}-l_{n}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}U_{n}\cdot\eta\,{\rm d}k\,{\rm d}x_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{n}\|_{L^{2}((R_{n}-l_{n},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N-1})^{3}}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}((R_{n}-l_{n},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N-1})^{3}}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty),

where the first equality follows from
v_{n}(x_{1},k)=0
for
x_{1}\leq R_{n}-l_{n}
, which is ensured by (
4.5
). The convergence step is concluded using (
4.14
) and the fact
R_{n}-l_{n}\to\infty
.

This finishes the proof of
\Sigma_{e,v}^{+}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}(L)
.

For
\omega\in\Sigma_{e,w}^{+}
we choose
k_{0}
and
w
like in the definition of
\Sigma_{e,w}^{+}
. After defining
w_{n}
by (
4.4
) with
v
replaced by
w
and the Weyl sequence
