U_{n}:=(0,0,w_{n})^{\top}
, the proof is a simplified version of the one above. Note, in particular, that the divergence condition is trivially satisfied by
U_{n}
.
∎

4.1.3
Periodic and Asymptotically Periodic Media

Let us first consider the case of
\perm_{+}(\cdot,\omega)
or
\perm_{-}(\cdot,\omega)
being periodic. As we show, a sufficient condition for (
4.3
) with respect to equation (
2.15
) or (
2.16
) is that the corresponding system is in the case of stability or conditional stability. See Sec.
2.2
for the stability concept.

If, in the case of periodic
\perm_{+}(\cdot,\omega)
, we have for the corresponding discriminants
D^{(v)}_{+}\in[-2,2]
or
D^{(w)}_{+}\in[-2,2]
, then there are bounded solutions of (
2.15
) or (
2.16
), resp., on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. If, on the other hand
\perm_{-}(\cdot,\omega)
is periodic and
D^{(v)}_{-}\in[-2,2]
or
D^{(w)}_{-}\in[-2,2]
, then there are bounded solutions on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\omega\in D(\perm)
and let
\perm_{+}(\cdot,\omega)
be
a
-periodic (with
a>0
). Assume that for some
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
one of the following two options holds.

a)

\omega\notin\Omega^{+}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}
and equation (
2.15
), with
\perm
replaced by
\perm_{+}
and
k
by
k_{0}
, is in the
case of stability or conditional stability, i.e.,
D_{+}^{(v)}(k_{0})\in[-2,2]
,

b)

(
2.16
), with
\perm
replaced by
\perm_{+}
and
k
by
k_{0}
, is in the
case of stability or conditional stability, i.e.,
D_{+}^{(w)}(k_{0})\in[-2,2]
.

Then
\omega\in\sweyl({\mathcal{L}})
.

An analogous statement holds if
\perm_{-}(\cdot,\omega)
is periodic using the conditions
D_{-}^{(v)}(k_{0})\in[-2,2]
and
D_{-}^{(w)}(k_{0})\in[-2,2]
, respectively.

Remark 4.8
.

Note that in Lemma
4.7
the periodicity of
\perm(\cdot,\omega)
needs to be satisfied on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
or on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
, not necessarily on both half lines. In fact, periodicity on
(x_{0},\infty)
or on
(-\infty,-x_{0})
with
|x_{0}|
large enough is sufficient as one easily sees in the construction of the Weyl sequence.

Proof.

We assume
a
-periodicity of
\perm_{+}
and first show that in the case a) we get
\omega\in\Sigma_{e,v}^{+}
. The statement then follows by Theorem
4.6
. First note that due to
D_{+}^{(v)}(k_{0})\in[-2,2]
a solution
v
of the homogeneous version of (
2.15
) with the form

v(x_{1})=e^{{\rm i}mx_{1}}p(x_{1})
exists, where
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
p
is
a-
periodic (cf. equation (
2.35
)). The solution
v
satisfies
v\in C_{b}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}})
.
Condition (
4.3
) is satisfied by choosing
R_{n}:=2na,l_{n}:=na
and
\varepsilon:=\tfrac{1}{2}\|p\|_{\infty}
. Indeed, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2l_{n}}\text{meas}\{x\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|v(x)|\geq\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2na}\text{meas}\{x\in[0,2na]:|p(x)|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|p\|_{\infty}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a}\text{meas}\{x\in[0,a]:|p(x)|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|p\|_{\infty}\},

which is positive since
p
is continuous.

The same argument applies in the case b) and leads to
\omega\in\Sigma_{e,w}^{+}
. The condition
\omega\notin\Omega_{|k_{0}|^{2}}^{+}
is not needed here because it does not appear in the definition of
\Sigma_{e,w}^{+}
.

Analogous arguments apply when
\perm_{-}(\cdot,\omega)
is periodic, leading to
\omega\in\Sigma_{e,v}^{-}
or
\omega\in\Sigma_{e,w}^{-}
, respectively.
∎

For the rest of Section
4.1
we consider
\perm
given by an
L^{1}
-perturbation of a periodic function. This can be understood as the case of an asymptotically periodic permittivity.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\omega\in D(\perm)
. Suppose that
\perm_{+}(\cdot,\omega)=\perm_{p,+}(\cdot,\omega)+\perm_{as,+}(\cdot,\omega)
, where
\perm_{p,+}(\cdot,\omega)
is
a
-periodic and
\perm_{as,+}(\cdot,\omega)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

a)

Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
be such that equation (
2.16
), with
\perm
replaced by
\perm_{p,+}
and
k
by
k_{0}
, is in the
case of stability, see Section
2.2
. Then
\omega\in\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
.

b)

Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
be such that equation (
2.15
), with
\perm
replaced by
\perm_{p,+}
and
k
by
k_{0}
, is in the case of stability. Moreover, suppose that
\left(\perm_{as,+}\right)^{\prime}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
and
\mbox{\rm dist}(\omega^{2}\perm_{p,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\omega),\{0,|k_{0}|^{2}\})>0
.
Then
\omega\in\sigma_{{\mathrm{W}eyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
.

Analogous statements hold if the corresponding assumptions are satisfied by
\perm_{-}
.

Remark 4.10
.

We note here that the medium does not need to be asymptotically periodic on both half lines
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
. Lemma
4.9
assumes this structure on either side of the interface. Moreover, for clarity, we remark that the conditions on on
\perm_{p,+}
in b) are equivalent to
\omega\notin\Omega^{+}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}\cup\Omega^{+}_{0}
with the
\Omega
-set defined based on the permittivity
\perm_{p,+}
.

Proof.

We restrict ourselves to the “+” case. For ease of notation we drop the subscript + and denote
x_{1}
simply by
x
in this proof.

a) Due to the fact that equation (
2.16
) is in the case of stability, there exists a fundamental system of the form
\psi_{1}(x)=e^{{\rm i}mx}p_{1}(x)
,
\psi_{2}(x)=e^{-{\rm i}mx}p_{2}(x)
with
m\in\mathbb{R}
and periodic
p_{1},p_{2}
(cf. equation (
2.35
)).

Let
w
denote the solution of the initial value problem

-w^{\prime\prime}+(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(\cdot,\omega))w=0,\ w(x_{0})=\psi_{1}(x_{0}),\ w^{\prime}(x_{0})=\psi^{\prime}_{1}(x_{0}),

(4.15)

where
x_{0}>0
will be specified later. Then
u=w-\psi_{1}
satisfies

-u^{\prime\prime}+(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{p}(\cdot,\omega))u=\omega^{2}\perm_{as}(\cdot,\omega)w,\ u(x_{0})=u^{\prime}(x_{0})=0,

whence the variation of constants formula gives

u(x)=\int_{x_{0}}^{x}\frac{\psi_{1}(x)\psi_{2}(t)-\psi_{2}(x)\psi_{1}(t)}{\psi_{1}(t)\psi^{\prime}_{2}(t)-\psi_{2}(t)\psi^{\prime}_{1}(t)}\omega^{2}\perm_{as}(t,\omega)w(t)\ dt

(4.16)

and

u^{\prime}(x)=\int_{x_{0}}^{x}\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{1}(x)\psi_{2}(t)-\psi^{\prime}_{2}(x)\psi_{1}(t)}{\psi_{1}(t)\psi^{\prime}_{2}(t)-\psi_{2}(t)\psi^{\prime}_{1}(t)}\omega^{2}\perm_{as}(t,\omega)w(t)\ dt.

(4.17)

Here, the Wronski determinant
\psi_{1}\psi^{\prime}_{2}-\psi_{2}\psi^{\prime}_{1}=p_{1}p_{2}^{\prime}-p_{2}p_{1}^{\prime}-2{\rm i}mp_{1}p_{2}
is periodic and hence, since it is non-zero, bounded away from
0
on
[0,\infty)
. Furthermore, the numerators in (
4.16
) and (
4.17
),

e^{{\rm i}m(x-t)}p_{1}(x)p_{2}(t)-e^{{\rm i}m(t-x)}p_{1}(t)p_{2}(x)

and

e^{{\rm i}m(x-t)}(p_{1}^{\prime}(x)+{\rm i}mp_{1}(x))p_{2}(t)-e^{{\rm i}m(t-x)}p_{1}(t)(p^{\prime}_{2}(x)-{\rm i}mp_{2}(x)),

respectively, are bounded on
[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
. Consequently, for some (
\omega
-dependent)
C>0
, by (
4.16
) and (
4.17
),

|u(x)|,|u^{\prime}(x)|\leq C\int_{x_{0}}^{x}|\perm_{as}(t,\omega)|\ dt\ \|w\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\hbox{ for }x\in[x_{0},\infty).

(4.18)

In particular, since
|w(x)|\leq|u(x)|+|\psi_{1}(x)|=|u(x)|+|p_{1}(x)|
, (
4.18
) implies

\|w\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\leq C\|\perm_{as}(\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},x)}\|w\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}+\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}

and hence
w
is bounded if

\|\perm_{as}(\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},\infty)}<\frac{1}{C}.

(4.19)

As
\perm_{as}(\cdot,\omega)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, (
4.19
) will be satisfied if
x_{0}
is chosen sufficiently large.
Now, (
4.18
) together with
|w^{\prime}(x)|\leq|u^{\prime}(x)|+|p^{\prime}_{1}(x)+{\rm i}mp_{1}(x)|
implies boundedness also of
w^{\prime}
. Thus,
w
is a solution of (
2.16
) with
k=k_{0}
in
C^{1}_{b}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
.

Moreover, again choosing
x_{0}
sufficiently large, we can arrange that

\|\perm_{as}(\cdot,\omega)\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},\infty)}\leq\frac{\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}}{4C\|w\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}}.

(4.20)

Then (
4.18
) together with
|w(x)|\geq|\psi_{1}(x)|-|u(x)|=|p_{1}(x)|-|u(x)|

gives

|w(x)|\geq|p_{1}(x)|-\frac{1}{4}\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)},\quad x\in[x_{0},\infty).

With
R_{n},l_{n}
chosen as in the proof of Lemma
4.7
, we have

\left\{x\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|w(x)|\geq\frac{1}{4}\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}\right\}\supseteq\left\{x\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|p_{1}(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}\right\}

provided that
R_{n}-l_{n}\geq x_{0}
, i.e.,
n\geq\frac{x_{0}}{a}
. Hence, using the calculation in the proof of Lemma
4.7
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2l_{n}}\mathrm{meas\ }

\displaystyle\left\{x\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|w(x)|\geq\frac{1}{4}\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2l_{n}}\mathrm{meas\ }\left\{x\in[R_{n}-l_{n},R_{n}+l_{n}]:|p_{1}(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a}\mathrm{meas\ }\left\{x\in[0,a]:|p_{1}(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}\right\}\ >\ 0

shows that condition (
4.3
) is satisfied. Theorem
4.6
implies
\omega\in\Sigma^{+}_{e,w}\subseteq\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
.

b) We use the fundamental system of (
2.15
) with
\perm
replaced by
\perm_{p}
of the form
\psi_{1}(x)=e^{{\rm i}mx}p_{1}(x)
,
\psi_{2}(x)=e^{-{\rm i}mx}p_{2}(x)
with
m\in\mathbb{R}
and periodic
p_{1},p_{2}
. Let
v
denote the solution of the initial value problem

-v^{\prime\prime}-\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}\perm^{\prime}(\cdot,\omega)}{\perm(\cdot,\omega)\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(\cdot,\omega)\right)}v^{\prime}+\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(\cdot,\omega)\right)v=0,\ v(x_{0})=\psi_{1}(x_{0}),\ v^{\prime}(x_{0})=\psi^{\prime}_{1}(x_{0}),

(4.21)

where
x_{0}\in[0,\infty)
will again be chosen later. Note that the differential equation in (
4.21
) is (
2.15
) with
k=k_{0}
and
r\equiv 0
. In what follows, we suppress the
\omega
-dependence of
\perm,\perm_{p}
and
\perm_{as}
. Now,
u=v-\psi_{1}
satisfies

\displaystyle-u^{\prime\prime}-\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}\perm^{\prime}_{p}}{\perm_{p}\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{p}\right)}u^{\prime}+\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{p}\right)u

\displaystyle=\omega^{2}\perm_{as}v+\left[\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}\perm^{\prime}}{\perm\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm\right)}-\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}\perm_{p}^{\prime}}{\perm_{p}\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{p}\right)}\right]v^{\prime},

(4.22)

\displaystyle u(x_{0})=u^{\prime}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=0.

We set

r:=\omega^{2}\perm_{as}v+\left[\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}\perm^{\prime}}{\perm\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm\right)}-\frac{|k_{0}|^{2}\perm_{p}^{\prime}}{\perm_{p}\left(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{p}\right)}\right]v^{\prime}.

(4.23)

Then, as in a), the variation of constants formula provides

|u(x)|,|u^{\prime}(x)|\leq C\int_{x_{0}}^{x}|r(t)|\ dt\hbox{ for }x\in[x_{0},\infty).

(4.24)

Since, by assumption,
\perm_{as}\in W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, the embedding
W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\hookrightarrow C_{b}[0,\infty)
implies boundedness of
\perm_{as}
and that
\perm_{as}(x)\to 0
as
x\to\infty
. Hence, our assumptions that
|\perm_{p}|\geq\delta
and
||k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{p}|\geq\delta
with some
\delta>0
imply the same inequalities on
[x_{0},\infty)
with
\perm_{p}
replaced by
\perm
and
\delta
by
\delta/2
when
x_{0}
is chosen sufficiently large. Thus,
r
given in (
4.23
) satisfies

|r|\leq C_{1}(|\perm_{as}|+|\perm^{\prime}_{as}|)(|v|+|v^{\prime}|)\hbox{ on }[x_{0},\infty),

(4.25)

which together with (
4.24
) and
u=v-\psi_{1}
gives, for
x\in(x_{0},\infty)
,

\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\leq CC_{1}\left(\int_{x_{0}}^{x}(|\perm_{as}|+|\perm^{\prime}_{as}|)\ dt\right)\left(\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}+\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\right)+\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}

and

\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\leq CC_{1}\left(\int_{x_{0}}^{x}(|\perm_{as}|+|\perm^{\prime}_{as}|)\ dt\right)\left(\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}+\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\right)+\|p^{\prime}_{1}+{\rm i}mp_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}.

Thus, choosing
x_{0}
such that

CC_{1}\left(\|\perm_{as}\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},\infty)}+\|\perm^{\prime}_{as}\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},\infty)}\right)\leq\frac{1}{3},

we obtain
v\in C^{1}_{b}[x_{0},\infty)
. Moreover, (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) show that

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\leq CC_{1}\left(\|\perm_{as}\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},\infty)}+\|\perm^{\prime}_{as}\|_{L^{1}(x_{0},\infty)}\right)\left(\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\right)

and hence for
x_{0}
large enough

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(x_{0},x)}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)}.

Therefore,

|v(x)|\geq|p_{1}(x)|-\frac{1}{4}\|p_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,a)},\ x\in[x_{0},\infty).

The rest of the proof is as in a) and we conclude
\omega\in\Sigma^{+}_{e,v}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
.
∎

4.2
Radiation along the Interface

The main building block of a Weyl sequence localized at the interface
x_{1}=0
is an eigenfunction of the operator

\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega):=\begin{pmatrix}|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(x_{1},\omega)&{\rm i}|k_{0}|\partial_{x_{1}}&0\\
{\rm i}|k_{0}|\partial_{x_{1}}&-\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(x_{1},\omega)&0\\
0&0&-\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}+|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(x_{1},\omega)\end{pmatrix}

with a fixed
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
and the domain

\displaystyle D(\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega))=

\displaystyle\left\{(u_{1},v,w)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}:v^{\prime}-{\rm i}|k_{0}|u_{1},v^{\prime\prime}-{\rm i}|k_{0}|u_{1}^{\prime},{\rm i}|k_{0}|v^{\prime}+|k_{0}|^{2}u_{1},w^{\prime},|k_{0}|w,w^{\prime\prime}-|k_{0}|^{2}w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm}),\right.

\displaystyle\left.\text{and}\ \eqref{E:divergence-uvw-1d}\ \text{and}\ \eqref{E:IFC-uvw-1d}\ \text{hold}\right\}.

(\perm(\cdot,\omega)u_{1})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k_{0}|\perm(\cdot,\omega)v=0\quad\text{on}\quad\mathbb{R}_{\pm},

(4.26)

\displaystyle\llbracket\perm(\cdot,\omega)u_{1}\rrbracket=\llbracket v\rrbracket=\llbracket v^{\prime}-{\rm i}|k_{0}|u_{1}\rrbracket=\llbracket w\rrbracket=\llbracket w^{\prime}\rrbracket=0.

(4.27)

Note that, unlike in (
1.19
), the notation
\llbracket f\rrbracket
in (
4.27
) denotes the difference of the classical limits
f(0+)-f(0-)
, which exist since all the arguments in (
4.27
) are in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
. Moreover,
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}
is an operator pencil with its spectrum defined analogously to that of
\mathcal{L}
.

Due to the decoupling of the system (
2.15
), (
2.16
) we find all eigenvalues
\omega
of
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}
by studying solutions of
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega)\psi=0
of the form
\psi=(u_{1},v,0)^{\top}
and those of the form
\psi=(0,0,w)^{\top}
.

For the former case we assume that there are fundamental systems of (
2.15
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
denoted by
\{v^{(1)}_{+},v^{(2)}_{+}\}
and on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
by
\{v^{(1)}_{-},v^{(2)}_{-}\}
with

v^{(1)}_{-}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}),\quad v^{(2)}_{-}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}),\quad v^{(1)}_{+}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),\quad\text{and}\quad v_{+}^{(2)}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})

(4.28)

and set

v:=\begin{cases}\alpha v^{(1)}_{-}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{-},\\
\beta v^{(2)}_{+}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{+},\end{cases}

and
u_{1}:=-\frac{{\rm i}|k_{0}|}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}
with
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{C}
. We consider
\omega\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(\Omega^{+}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}\cup\Omega^{-}_{|k_{0}|^{2}})
. Then we have
u_{1}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
and
(u_{1},v)
satisfies all
L^{2}
-conditions in
D(\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega))
. For
k_{0}\neq 0
the interface conditions
\llbracket v\rrbracket=\llbracket\perm u_{1}\rrbracket=0
read

\begin{pmatrix}-v^{(1)}_{-}(0-)&v^{(2)}_{+}(0+)\\
-\frac{\perm(0-)}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(0-)}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0-)&\frac{\perm(0+)}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(0+)}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0+)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
\beta\end{pmatrix}=0.

(4.29)

The last interface condition
\llbracket{\rm i}|k_{0}|u_{1}-v^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
follows from the second equation in (
4.29
). For
k_{0}=0
the condition
\llbracket\perm u_{1}\rrbracket=0
holds trivially and
\llbracket v\rrbracket=\llbracket{\rm i}|k_{0}|u_{1}-v^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
reduces to

\begin{pmatrix}-v^{(1)}_{-}(0-)&v^{(2)}_{+}(0+)\\
-v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0-)&v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0+)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
\beta\end{pmatrix}=0.

(4.30)

In summary, the interface conditions (
2.3
) can be satisfied by
v\neq 0
if and only if

\det\begin{pmatrix}v^{(1)}_{-}(0-)&v^{(2)}_{+}(0+)\\
\frac{\perm(0-)}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(0-)}v^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0-)&\frac{\perm(0+)}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm(0+)}v^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0+)\end{pmatrix}=0.

(4.31)

Note that condition (
4.31
) is the same as
d(k_{0})=0
for
d
defined in (
3.2
) - however with the fundamental systems satisfying slightly weaker conditions than in Section
3
.

Finally, because
(\perm u_{1})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k_{0}|\perm v={\rm i}|k_{0}|\left(-\left(\frac{\perm}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}+\perm v\right)
, the divergence condition (
4.26
) follows from (
2.15
).

Similarly, for eigenfunctions of the form
\psi=(0,0,w)^{\top}
, we assume that there are fundamental systems of (
2.16
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
denoted by
\{w^{(1)}_{+},w^{(2)}_{+}\}
and on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
by
\{w^{(1)}_{-},w^{(2)}_{-}\}
with

w^{(1)}_{-}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}),\ w^{(2)}_{-}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-}),\ \ w^{(1)}_{+}\notin L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),\quad\text{and}\quad w^{(2)}_{+}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})

(4.32)

and set

w:=\begin{cases}\alpha w^{(1)}_{-}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{-},\\
\beta w^{(2)}_{+}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{+}\end{cases}

with
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{C}
. The interface conditions
\llbracket w\rrbracket=\llbracket w^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
are

\begin{pmatrix}-w^{(1)}_{-}(0-)&w^{(2)}_{+}(0+)\\
-w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0-)&w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0+)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
\beta\end{pmatrix}=0.

(4.33)

They can be satisfied with
w\neq 0
if and only if

\det\begin{pmatrix}w^{(1)}_{-}(0-)&w^{(2)}_{+}(0+)\\
w^{(1)^{\prime}}_{-}(0-)&w^{(2)^{\prime}}_{+}(0+)\end{pmatrix}=0.

(4.34)

The divergence condition (
4.26
) is trivially satisfied since
u_{1}=v=0
.

Note again that condition (
4.34
) is the same as
\widetilde{d}(k_{0})=0
for
\widetilde{d}
defined in (
3.4
) - up to the slightly different conditions on the fundamental system.

In summary, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 4.11
.

Assume (
1.3
), let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
and let
\{v^{(1)}_{\pm},v^{(2)}_{\pm}\}
and
\{w^{(1)}_{\pm},w^{(2)}_{\pm}\}
denote fundamental systems of (
2.15
) and (
2.16
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
with
k=k_{0}
, respectively, satisfying the properties (
4.28
) and (
4.32
), resp. Define

N^{(v)}_{k_{0}}:=\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus(\Omega^{+}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}\cup\Omega^{-}_{|k_{0}|^{2}}):\ \eqref{E:L2-cond-v}\ \text{and}\ \eqref{E:det-v-eq}\ \text{hold}\}

(4.35)

and

N^{(w)}_{k_{0}}:=\{\omega\in D(\perm):\eqref{E:L2-cond-w}\ \text{and}\ \eqref{E:det-w-eq}\ \text{hold}\}.

(4.36)

Then

N^{(v)}_{k_{0}}\cup N^{(w)}_{k_{0}}\subset\sigma_{p}(\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}).

Corresponding to
\omega\in N^{(v)}_{k_{0}}
there is a solution

(u_{1},v,w)^{\top}=(\tfrac{-{\rm i}|k_{0}|v^{\prime}}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm},v,0)^{\top}=:\psi^{(v)}

(4.37)

of the homogenous version of (
2.15
)-(
2.17
), (
2.3
), and (
4.26
) with
v
given by

v:=\begin{cases}\alpha v^{(1)}_{-}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{-},\\
\beta v^{(2)}_{+}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{+},\end{cases}

(4.38)

where
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
solves (
4.29
).

Corresponding to
\omega\in N^{(w)}_{k_{0}}
there is a solution

(u_{1},v,w)^{\top}=(0,0,w)^{\top}=:\psi^{(w)}

(4.39)

of the same system with
w
given by

w:=\begin{cases}\alpha w^{(1)}_{-}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{-},\\
\beta w^{(2)}_{+}&\text{on}\ \mathbb{R}_{+}\end{cases}

(4.40)

where
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
solves (
4.33
).

Remark 4.12
.

Note that because the dependence on
k_{0}
in all of (
2.15
)-(
2.17
), (
2.3
), and (
4.26
) is only via the absolute value
|k_{0}|
, we have

N_{k_{0}}^{(v)}=N_{l}^{(v)}\ \text{and}\ N_{k_{0}}^{(w)}=N_{l}^{(w)}\ \text{for all}\ |k_{0}|=|l|.

As we show next, the solution
(u_{1},v,w)
corresponding to
\omega\in N^{(v)}_{k_{0}}
for some
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
and the solution corresponding to
\omega\in N^{(w)}_{k_{0}}
for some
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
can be used to generate a Weyl sequence for the operator pencil
{\mathcal{L}}
. Since this can be carried out for any
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
, it makes sense to study the unions

N^{(v)}:=\bigcup_{k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}N^{(v)}_{k_{0}}\ \text{and }\ N^{(w)}:=\bigcup_{k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}N^{(w)}_{k_{0}}.

(4.41)

Theorem 4.13
.

We have

N^{(v)}\cup N^{(w)}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}}).

Proof.

With the help of Lemmas
2.1
and
2.2
we work only with the operator
\widetilde{L}(\omega)
. We divide the proof into three parts. In the parts 1) and 2) we study the case
\omega\in N^{(v)}
and in the last part we study
\omega\in N^{(w)}
.

1) First, we assume
\omega\in N^{(v)}_{k_{0}}
with
k_{0}\neq 0
. The corresponding eigenfunction of
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega)
is denoted by
\psi^{(v)}
, see (
4.37
). Recall that the last component of
\psi^{(v)}
is zero.
To construct the Weyl sequence
\xi_{n}=(u_{1,n},v_{n},w_{n})^{\top}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we first choose an arbitrary
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\setminus\{0\}
and define
\theta_{n}=(\theta_{1,n},\theta_{v,n},\theta_{w,n})^{\top}
,

\theta_{n}(x_{1},k):=e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}|k|^{2}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\psi^{(v)}(x_{1})+\widehat{r}_{n}(x_{1},k),

(4.42)

where
k=k_{2}
if
N=2
,
k=(k_{2},k_{3})
if
N=3
,
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1},\mathbb{R})
, and

\widehat{r}_{n}(x_{1},k):=\left(0,-n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}|k|(|k|-|k_{0}|)e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\psi^{(v)}_{2}(x_{1}),0\right)^{\top}.

Note that
\theta_{n}
is the Fourier transform (with respect to
x_{\parallel}
) of

\displaystyle\widecheck{\theta}_{n}(x_{1},x_{\parallel})=\penalty

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}\left(|k_{0}|^{2}\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-\zeta n^{2}}{n}\right)-2{\rm i}n^{-1}k_{0}\cdot\nabla\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-\zeta n^{2}}{n}\right)-n^{-2}\Delta\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-\zeta n^{2}}{n}\right)\right)e^{{\rm i}k_{0}\cdot x_{\parallel}}\psi^{(v)}(x_{1})

\displaystyle+r_{n}(x_{1},x_{\parallel}),

where
x_{\parallel}=x_{2}
if
N=2
and
x_{\parallel}=(x_{2},x_{3})
if
N=3
. The correction
r_{n}
ensures that
\theta_{n}
satisfies the divergence condition. Indeed, since
(\perm\psi^{(v)}_{1})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k_{0}|\perm\psi^{(v)}_{2}=0
, we get

(\perm\theta_{1,n})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k|\perm\theta_{v,n}=\perm\left({\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)}_{2}+{\rm i}|k|\widehat{r}_{n,2}\right)=0,

where

\alpha_{n}(k):=e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}|k|^{2}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0})).

The interface conditions
\llbracket\theta_{w,n}\rrbracket=\llbracket\partial_{x_{1}}\theta_{w,n}\rrbracket=0
hold automatically because
\theta_{w,n}=0
. The conditions
\llbracket\theta_{v,n}\rrbracket=\llbracket\perm\theta_{1,n}\rrbracket=0
are satisfied too. This is because
\theta_{v,n}=\alpha_{n}\frac{|k_{0}|}{|k|}\psi^{(v)}_{2}
and
\theta_{1,n}=\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)}_{1}
and we have
\llbracket\psi^{(v)}_{2}\rrbracket=\llbracket\perm\psi^{(v)}_{1}\rrbracket=0
.

The remaining interface condition in (
4.27
) is not satisfied as

\llbracket{\rm i}|k|\theta_{1,n}-\partial_{x_{1}}\theta_{v,n}\rrbracket=\alpha_{n}\frac{|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2}}{|k||k_{0}|}\left\llbracket\partial_{x_{1}}\psi^{(v)}_{2}\right\rrbracket=:\gamma_{n}(k),

where we have used the fact that
\llbracket\perm\psi_{1}^{(v)}\rrbracket=0
and
(|k_{0}|^{2}-W)\psi_{1}{(v)}+{\rm i}|k_{0}|\partial_{x_{1}}\psi_{2}^{(v)}=0
.
For future reference, we note that

\gamma_{n}(k)=n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}\frac{|k|(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})}{|k_{0}|}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}\left\llbracket\partial_{x_{1}}\psi^{(v)}_{2}\right\rrbracket.

(4.43)

The Weyl sequence
\xi_{n}
will be chosen by introducing a correction term in such a way that this interface condition is satisfied (and all other conditions remain to hold). We set

\xi_{n}=(u_{1,n},v_{n},w_{n})^{\top}:=c_{n}(\theta_{n}-\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}),\quad\widehat{s}=(\widehat{s}_{1},\widehat{s}_{2},0)^{\top},

(4.44)

with
\widehat{s}
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}(\widehat{s}_{1}\perm)+{\rm i}\perm|k|\widehat{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(4.45)

\displaystyle\llbracket\widehat{s}_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=0,

(4.46)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\rm i}|k|\widehat{s}_{1}-\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{2}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=1,

(4.47)

\displaystyle\llbracket\perm\widehat{s}_{1}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=0.

(4.48)

The constants
c_{n}>0
are selected so that
\|\xi_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=1
for all
n
.

For (
4.45
) we set

\widehat{s}_{2}:=\frac{{\rm i}}{|k|\perm}\partial_{x_{1}}(\widehat{s}_{1}\perm).

(4.49)

The following
\widehat{s}_{1}
is one possible choice satisfying (
4.46
)-(
4.48
), as one easily checks.

\widehat{s}_{1}=\begin{cases}\left(\perm_{-}^{-1}+a(k)x_{1}^{2}\right)\eta(x_{1}),&x_{1}<0,\\
\perm_{+}^{-1}\eta(x_{1}),&x_{1}>0,\end{cases}

(4.50)

where
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
\eta(0)=1,\eta^{\prime}(0)=\eta^{\prime\prime}(0)=0
and
a(k):=-\frac{|k|}{2}\left({\rm i}+|k|\llbracket\perm^{-1}\rrbracket\right)
.

As a preliminary step before checking the
L^{2}
-conditions, we study
\widehat{s}
and the derivatives
\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}
,
\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{2}
, and
\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widehat{s}_{2}
, which appear later in
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\widehat{s}
. First, we have

\|\widehat{s}_{1}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}\leq\|\perm_{-}^{-1}\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}+|a(k)|\|x_{1}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}\leq c(1+|k|^{2})

and

\|\widehat{s}_{1}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\|\perm_{+}^{-1}\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq c

producing

\|\widehat{s}_{1}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq c(1+|k|^{2}).

(4.51)

For
\widehat{s}_{2}
we have

\widehat{s}_{2}(x_{1},k)=\begin{cases}{\rm i}|k|^{-1}\perm_{-}^{-1}(x_{1})\left(\eta^{\prime}(x_{1})+a(k)\partial_{x_{1}}(\perm_{-}(x_{1})x_{1}^{2}\eta(x_{1}))\right),&x_{1}<0,\\
{\rm i}|k|^{-1}\perm_{+}^{-1}(x_{1})\eta^{\prime}(x_{1}),&x_{1}>0,\end{cases}

hence

\|\widehat{s}_{2}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})}\leq c|k|^{-1}(1+|k|^{2})=c(|k|^{-1}+|k|),\qquad\|\widehat{s}_{2}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq c|k|^{-1},

resulting in

\|\widehat{s}_{2}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq c(|k|^{-1}+|k|).

(4.52)

For the derivatives, we calculate

\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}(x_{1},k)=\begin{cases}\left(2a(k)x_{1}-\frac{\perm_{-}^{\prime}(x_{1})}{\perm_{-}^{2}(x_{1})}\right)\eta(x_{1})+\left(\perm_{-}^{-1}+a(k)x_{1}^{2}\right)\eta^{\prime}(x_{1}),&x_{1}<0,\\
-\frac{\perm_{+}^{\prime}(x_{1})}{\perm_{+}^{2}(x_{1})}\eta(x_{1})+\perm_{+}^{-1}(x_{1})\eta^{\prime}(x_{1}),&x_{1}>0.\end{cases}

Due to the definition of
\widehat{s}_{2}
and

\partial_{x_{1}}(\perm\widehat{s}_{1})(x_{1},k)=-\frac{{\rm i}\perm^{\prime}(x_{1})}{|k|\perm^{2}(x_{1})}\partial_{x_{1}}(\perm\widehat{s}_{1})(x_{1},k)+\frac{{\rm i}}{|k|\perm(x_{1})}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}(\perm\widehat{s}_{1})(x_{1},k),

one can easily observe that
\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\widehat{s}_{2}(x_{1},k),m=1,2
, have the form

\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{2}(x_{1},k)=\begin{cases}|k|^{-1}(f_{1}(x_{1})+g_{1}(x_{1})a(k)),&x_{1}<0,\\
|k|^{-1}h_{1}(x_{1}),&x_{1}>0\end{cases}

and

\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widehat{s}_{2}(x_{1},k)=\begin{cases}|k|^{-1}(f_{2}(x_{1})+g_{2}(x_{1})a(k)),&x_{1}<0,\\
|k|^{-1}h_{2}(x_{1}),&x_{1}>0\end{cases}

with some functions
f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
and
h_{1},h_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. The
L^{2}
-property of these functions follows from
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
\perm\in W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{\pm})
. Indeed, derivatives of
\perm
of order one, two, and three appear in these functions.

As a result we obtain

\|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq c(1+|k|^{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\|\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\widehat{s}_{2}(\cdot,k)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq c(|k|^{-1}+|k|),m=1,2.

(4.53)

For the
L^{2}
-estimates of
\xi_{n}
(and
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}
) it is useful to understand
\||k|^{p}\gamma_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}
for
p\in\mathbb{Z},p\geq-1
. For such
p
we have

\displaystyle\||k|^{p}\gamma_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\left|\left\llbracket\partial_{x_{1}}\psi_{2}^{(v)}\right\rrbracket\right|^{2}\frac{n^{N-1}}{|k_{0}|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|k|^{2+2p}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})^{2}|\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))|^{2}\,{\rm d}k

(4.54)

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}(|k_{0}|+|\tfrac{\kappa}{n}|)^{2+2p}(2n^{-1}|\kappa||k_{0}|+n^{-2}|\kappa|^{2})^{2}|\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa

\displaystyle\leq cn^{-2}\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{3+p}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})},

where we have used the fact
\|f\|^{2}_{H^{j}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}(1+|k|^{j})^{2}|\widehat{f}(k)|^{2}\,{\rm d}k
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let us now check the
L^{2}
-conditions in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}(\omega)
. First, we show that
\xi_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
and that
m\leq c_{n}\leq M
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
with some
m>0
and
M>0
independent of
n
. This will follow from
\psi^{(v)}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
, from
\widehat{r}_{n},\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}\to 0
in
L^{2}
and from upper and lower bounds on
\|\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}
.

Since
\widehat{r}_{n,2}(x_{1},k)=-n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}|k|(|k|-|k_{0}|)\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\psi_{2}^{(v)}(x_{1})e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}
and
\widehat{r}_{n,1}=\widehat{r}_{n,3}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{r}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\psi_{2}^{(v)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|k_{0}+\tfrac{\kappa}{n}|^{2}(|k_{0}+\tfrac{\kappa}{n}|-|k_{0}|)^{2}|\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa

(4.55)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-2}\|\psi_{2}^{(v)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|k_{0}+\tfrac{\kappa}{n}|^{2}|\kappa|^{2}|\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa\leq cn^{-2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}\to 0\ (n\to\infty).

Note that
\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}<\infty
since
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
.

For
\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}
we use (
4.51
), (
4.52
), and (
4.54
) to conclude

\|\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq cn^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0\ (n\to\infty).

(4.56)

Next, we show the boundedness of
\|\alpha_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}
from above and away from zero. Because
\alpha_{n}(k)=n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}|k|^{2}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}
, using the dominated convergence, we obtain

\frac{1}{2}|k_{0}|^{4}\|\widehat{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}\leq\|\alpha_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|k_{0}+\tfrac{\kappa}{n}|^{4}|\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa\leq 2|k_{0}|^{4}\|\widehat{\varphi}\|^{2}_{L_{2}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}

(4.57)

for all
n
large enough.

Estimates (
4.55
), (
4.56
), and (
4.57
) imply
\xi_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
and
m\leq c_{n}\leq M
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
with some
m
and
M
independent of
n
.

We continue by checking the remaining
L^{2}
-conditions on
\xi_{n}
in order to belong to
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
. Because
w_{n}=0
, it is clearly sufficient to show

|k|^{2}u_{1,n},|k|\partial_{x_{1}}u_{1,n},\partial_{x_{1}}v_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}v_{n},|k|\partial_{x_{1}}v_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}).

Note that
|k|^{2}u_{1,n},\partial_{x_{1}}v_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
implies also
\partial_{x_{1}}v_{n}-{\rm i}|k|u_{1,n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
.
As
\widehat{\varphi}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
and because every component in
\xi_{n}
includes
\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))
as a factor, we automatically get
|k|^{2}u_{1,n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. For the same reason,
|k|\partial_{x_{1}}\xi_{n,j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
if
\partial_{x_{1}}\xi_{n,j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
. Hence, we only need to verify that

\partial_{x_{1}}u_{1,n},\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}v_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})\quad\text{for}\quad m=1,2.

We have

\partial_{x_{1}}u_{1,n}=c_{n}(\alpha_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}\psi_{1}^{(v)}-\gamma_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}),

where

\partial_{x_{1}}\psi_{1}^{(v)}=-{\rm i}|k_{0}|\left(\frac{\psi^{(v)^{\prime\prime}}_{2}}{|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}+\frac{\omega^{2}\perm^{\prime}}{(|k_{0}|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)^{2}}\psi^{(v)^{\prime}}_{2}\right),

which is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
due to
\psi_{2}^{(v)}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\oplus H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
(because
\psi^{(v)}\in D(\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega))
),
\perm\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\oplus W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
, and
\omega\notin\Omega
. Together with
\alpha_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
this implies
\alpha_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}\psi^{(v)}_{1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. For the second term, i.e.,
\gamma_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}
, we use (
4.53
) and (
4.54
) to obtain
\|\gamma_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq cn^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}
.

Next,

\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}v_{n}=c_{n}(\alpha_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\psi_{2}^{(v)}+\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\widehat{r}_{n,2}-\gamma_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\widehat{s}_{2}),\ m=1,2.

The first term is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
due to
\psi_{2}^{(v)}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\oplus H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
and
\alpha_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
. For the second term we have

\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\widehat{r}_{n,2}(x_{1},k)=-n|k|(|k|-|k_{0}|)e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\psi^{(v)}_{2}(x_{1}),

which is again in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
using, in addition,
\widehat{\varphi}\in\mathcal{S}
. For the third term we use
(
4.53
) and (
4.54
) to estimate

\|\gamma_{n}\partial_{x_{1}}^{m}\widehat{s}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq cn^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})},m=1,2.

We conclude that
\xi_{n}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Next, we show
\check{\xi}_{n}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L^{2}
. Thanks to (
4.55
), (
4.56
), and the boundedness of
(c_{n})
it is sufficient to show

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}(x_{\parallel})\psi^{(v)}_{j}(x_{1})f(x_{1},x_{\parallel})\,{\rm d}(x_{1},x_{\parallel})\to 0\ (n\to\infty)\quad\text{for all}\quad f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})\ \text{and}\ j=1,2,

(4.58)

where
\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}
is the inverse Fourier transformation of
\alpha_{n}
, i.e. (see the formula for
\widecheck{\theta}_{n}
)

\displaystyle\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}(x_{\parallel})

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}e^{{\rm i}k_{0}\cdot x_{\parallel}}\left(|k_{0}|^{2}\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)-2{\rm i}n^{-1}k_{0}\cdot\nabla\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)-n^{-2}\Delta\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)\right),

where we recall that
x_{\parallel}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
. Because
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
, we only need to show for
j=1,2

I_{j,n}:=n^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\mu\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)\psi^{(v)}_{j}(x_{1})f(x_{1},x_{\parallel})\,{\rm d}x\to 0\ (n\to\infty)\quad\text{for all}\quad f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}),\mu\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1}).

For any
\mu\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})

\displaystyle\left|n^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}\mu\left(\frac{y-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)f(x_{1},y)\,{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle=\left|n^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}\int_{nK+n^{2}\zeta}\mu\left(\frac{y-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)f(x_{1},y)\,{\rm d}y\right|

(4.59)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu\|_{L^{2}(K)}\|f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(nK+n^{2}\zeta)}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu\|_{L^{2}(K)}\|f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\setminus[-n,n]^{N-1})}\to 0\ (n\to\infty),

where
K:=\mbox{\rm supp}(\mu)
and the last inequality holds for all
n
large enough. Hence (with the help of Fubini’s theorem)

\displaystyle|I_{j,n}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu\|_{L^{2}(K)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\psi^{(v)}_{j}(x_{1})|\|f(x_{1},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\setminus[-n,n]^{N-1})}\,{\rm d}x_{1}

(4.60)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu\|_{L^{2}(K)}\|\psi_{j}^{(v)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}\setminus(\mathbb{R}\times[-n,n]^{N-1}))}\to 0

It remains to verify
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}\to 0
in
L^{2}
. As seen in (
4.44
) and (
4.42
),
\xi_{n}
consists of three terms. We study these separately.
From

\widetilde{L}(\omega)\widehat{r}_{n}=-n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}|k|(|k|-|k_{0}|)e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\begin{pmatrix}{\rm i}|k|\partial_{x_{1}}\psi^{(v)}_{2}\\
-\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\psi^{(v)}_{2}-W\psi^{(v)}_{2}\\
0\end{pmatrix}

one obtains similarly to (
4.55
)

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)\widehat{r}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}^{2}\leq cn^{-2}\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}(\|\psi^{(v)}_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|\psi^{(v)}_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})})\to 0\quad(n\to\infty).

Next,

\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\gamma_{n}\widehat{s})=\gamma_{n}\begin{pmatrix}(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)\widehat{s}_{1}+{\rm i}|k|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{2}\\
{\rm i}|k|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}-(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}+\omega^{2}\perm)\widehat{s}_{2}\\
0\end{pmatrix}

Using again (
4.51
), (
4.52
), (
4.53
), and (
4.54
), we get

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\gamma_{n}\widehat{s})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq cn^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{7}(\mathbb{R})}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty).

The last term in
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}
is
\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})=\alpha_{n}\widetilde{L}(\omega)\psi^{(v)}
. Because
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega)\psi^{(v)}=0
, we have

\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})=\alpha_{n}\begin{pmatrix}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})\psi^{(v)}_{1}+{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)\partial_{x_{1}}\psi^{(v)}_{2}\\
{\rm i}(|k|-|k_{0}|)\partial_{x_{1}}\psi^{(v)}_{1}\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|\alpha_{n}(k)|^{2}(|k|-|k_{0}|)^{2}\,{\rm d}k\leq n^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|k_{0}+\tfrac{\kappa}{n}|^{4}|\kappa|^{2}|\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa\to 0\quad(n\to\infty),

analogously
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|\alpha_{n}(k)|^{2}(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})^{2}\,{\rm d}k\to 0
, and
\psi^{(v)}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\oplus H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
, we also get that
\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})\to 0
in
L^{2}
.

In summary,
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}\to 0
in
L^{2}
.

2) Let us now choose
\omega\in N^{(v)}_{0}
. As Lemma
4.11
shows, there is a corresponding eigenfunction which has the form

\psi^{(v)}=(0,v_{0},0)^{\top},

where
v_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
solves
v_{0}^{\prime\prime}+\omega^{2}\perm(x,\omega)v_{0}=0
. Like in part 1) we choose
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
and
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
arbitrary. The naive choice

e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}k\cdot\zeta}n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}\widehat{\varphi}(nk)\psi^{(v)}(x_{1})

does not satisfy the divergence condition (
4.26
). To correct for this, we choose

\theta_{n}(x_{1},k):=(\theta_{1,n},\theta_{v,n},\theta_{w,n})^{\top}(x_{1},k):=\alpha_{n}(k)\psi^{(v)}(x_{1})+\widehat{r}_{n}(x_{1},k),

(4.61)

where

\alpha_{n}(k):=e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}k\cdot\zeta}n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}\widehat{\varphi}(nk)\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{r}_{n}(x_{1},k):=\left({\rm i}|k|\perm^{-1}(x_{1})\omega^{-2}\alpha_{n}(k)v_{0}^{\prime}(x_{1}),0,0\right)^{\top}.

Clearly,
\theta_{n}
satisfies the divergence condition
(\perm\theta_{n,1})^{\prime}+{\rm i}|k|\perm\theta_{v,n}=0

because
v_{0}^{\prime\prime}+\omega^{2}\perm(x,\omega)v_{0}=0
.

The interface conditions
\llbracket\theta_{w,n}\rrbracket=\llbracket\partial_{x_{1}}\theta_{w,n}\rrbracket=0
hold trivially. The condition
\llbracket\theta_{v,n}\rrbracket=0
holds because
\llbracket\psi^{(v)}_{2}\rrbracket=\llbracket v_{0}\rrbracket=0
. The condition
\llbracket\perm\theta_{1,n}\rrbracket=0
is satisfied since
v_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and hence
\llbracket v_{0}^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
. Like in part 1) the remaining interface condition in (
4.27
) is not satisfied. Indeed, using again
\llbracket v_{0}^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
, we get

\llbracket{\rm i}|k|\theta_{1,n}-\partial_{x_{1}}\theta_{v,n}\rrbracket={\rm i}|k|\llbracket\theta_{1,n}\rrbracket=-|k|^{2}\alpha_{n}(k)\omega^{-2}\llbracket\perm^{-1}v_{0}^{\prime}\rrbracket=:\gamma_{n}(k)\neq 0.

(4.62)

Analogously to part 1) we propose the Weyl sequence

\xi_{n}=(u_{1,n},v_{n},w_{n})^{\top}:=c_{n}(\theta_{n}-\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}),\quad\widehat{s}=(\widehat{s}_{1},\widehat{s}_{2},0)^{\top},

with
\theta_{n}
in (
4.61
),
\gamma_{n}
in (
4.62
),
\widehat{s}_{1}
in (
4.50
),
\widehat{s}_{2}
in (
4.49
), and
c_{n}>0
such that
\|\xi_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}=1
for all
n
.

For
\xi_{n}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
it remains to check the
L^{2}
-conditions in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}(\omega)
. We start again by showing that
\xi_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
and that
m\leq c_{n}\leq M
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
with some
m>0
and
M>0
independent of
n
. First, one easily obtains

\||k|^{p}\alpha_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\leq n^{-p}\|\varphi\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\forall p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

(4.63)

This, together with
v_{0}^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\omega\notin\Omega_{\delta}
, implies

\|\widehat{r}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq cn^{-1}\|v_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty).

(4.64)

Analogously to (
4.54
), we have for
\gamma_{n}
from (
4.62
) and
p\in\mathbb{Z},p\geq-1

\displaystyle\||k|^{p}\gamma_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\left|\left\llbracket\perm^{-1}v_{0}^{\prime}\right\rrbracket\right|^{2}n^{N-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|k|^{4+2p}|\widehat{\varphi}(nk)|^{2}\,{\rm d}k

(4.65)

\displaystyle\leq cn^{-4-2p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|\kappa|^{4+2p}\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}\,{\rm d}\kappa\leq cn^{-4-2p}\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{2+p}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}.

Hence, using again the estimates (
4.51
) and (
4.52
) of
\|s_{1,2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
, we obtain

\|\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq cn^{-2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0,\quad\|\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq cn^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty).

(4.66)

As a result we have

\frac{1}{2}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq\|\theta_{n}-\gamma_{n}\widehat{s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq 2\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}

for all
n
large enough and we conclude that
m\leq c_{n}\leq M
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
with some
m>0
and
M>0
independent of
n
.

Just like in the case
k_{0}\neq 0
, the remaining
L^{2}
-conditions to be checked for
\xi_{n}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
are

|k|^{2}u_{1,n},|k|\partial_{x_{1}}u_{1,n},\partial_{x_{1}}v_{n},\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}v_{n},|k|\partial_{x_{1}}v_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}).

These hold analogously to the case
k_{0}\neq 0
using the fact
v_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and the estimates (
4.63
) as well as (
4.65
) together with (
4.51
), (
4.52
), and (
4.53
).

Next, we show
\check{\xi}_{n}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L^{2}
as
n\to\infty
. Due to (
4.64
) and (
4.66
) it remains to check
\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)}\rightharpoonup 0
, i.e., to show (
4.58
) with
\alpha_{n}(k):=e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}k\cdot\zeta}n^{\frac{N-1}{2}}\widehat{\varphi}(nk)
and
\psi^{(v)}=(0,v_{0},0)^{\top}
.
We have

\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}(x_{\parallel})=n^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-n^{2}\zeta}{n}\right)

and the same argument as in (
4.60
) shows
\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)}\rightharpoonup 0
.

In the last step, we check that
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}\to 0
in
L^{2}
as
n\to\infty
. Firstly, we have

\widetilde{L}(\omega)\widehat{r}_{n}=\alpha_{n}(k)v_{0}^{\prime}\perm^{-1}\omega^{-2}\left((|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm){\rm i}|k|,-|k|^{2},0\right)^{\top}.

As
\omega\notin\Omega
, we get

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)\widehat{r}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq cn^{-1}\|v_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|\varphi\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0

using (
4.63
). Secondly,

\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\gamma_{n}\widehat{s})=\gamma_{n}\left((|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)\widehat{s}_{1}+{\rm i}|k|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{2},{\rm i}|k|\partial_{x_{1}}\widehat{s}_{1}-(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}+\omega^{2}\perm)\widehat{s}_{2},0\right)^{\top}.

Combining again (
4.51
), (
4.52
), and (
4.53
) with (
4.54
), we get

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\gamma_{n}\widehat{s})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}\leq cn^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0.

To complete this part of the proof, we show that
\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})\to 0
in
L^{2}
. Indeed, as

\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})={\rm i}|k|\alpha_{n}\left(v_{0}^{\prime},-v_{0}^{\prime\prime}-\omega^{2}\perm v_{0},0\right)^{\top},

one gets with (
4.63
)

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq cn^{-1}\|v_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\to 0

and
\widetilde{L}(\omega)(\alpha_{n}\psi^{(v)})_{2}=0
.

3) Finally, we consider
\omega\in N^{(w)}
. Hence, there exists
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
such that
\omega\in N^{(w)}_{k_{0}}
. A corresponding eigenfunction of
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega)
is denoted by
\psi^{(w)}
, see (
4.39
). Recall that the first two components of
\psi^{(w)}
are zero.

We choose the Weyl sequence
\xi_{n}=(0,0,w_{n})^{\top},n\in\mathbb{N},
as

\xi_{n}(x_{1},k_{2},k_{3}):=cn^{\frac{N-1}{2}}e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\psi^{(w)}(x_{1}),

where
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\setminus\{0\}
and
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1},\mathbb{R})
are arbitrary and
c=(\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})}\|\psi^{(w)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})})^{-1}
so that
\|\xi_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})}=1,
which is checked by an easy calculation. Note that
\xi_{n}
is the Fourier transform of

\check{\xi}_{n}(x_{1},x_{\parallel})=cn^{-\frac{N-1}{2}}e^{{\rm i}k_{0}\cdot x_{\parallel}}\varphi\left(\frac{x_{\parallel}-\zeta n^{2}}{n}\right)\psi^{(w)}(x_{1}).

The divergence condition and all interface conditions are satisfied by
\xi_{n}
. In detail, the divergence condition as well as the interface conditions involving
u_{1}
and
v
are automatic because the first two components of
\xi_{n}
are zero. And the interface conditions
\llbracket w\rrbracket=\llbracket w^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
hold because they are satisfied by the third component of
\psi^{(w)}
.

To conclude that
\xi_{n}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\omega}
, the
L^{2}
-conditions in
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
must be checked.
\xi_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
was discussed above. Since
\psi^{(w)}_{1,2}=0
, the only remaining
L^{2}
-properties are
\partial_{x_{1}}w_{n},|k|w_{n},\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}w_{n}-|k|^{2}w_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
. These all follow from
\psi^{(w)}_{3}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\oplus H^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{-})
and from
\widehat{\varphi}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{N-1})
.

The weak convergence
\xi_{n}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L^{2}
is shown analogously to (
4.59
) and (
4.60
) leading to

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\check{\xi}_{n,3}(x)f(x)\,{\rm d}x\right|\leq c\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(K)}\|\psi^{(w)}_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}\setminus(\mathbb{R}\times[-n,n]^{N-1}))}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty)

for every
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
, where
K:=\mbox{\rm supp}(\varphi)
.

Finally, for
\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}\to 0
we use again that
\widetilde{L}_{k_{0}}(\omega)\psi^{(w)}=0
to get

\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}=cn^{\frac{N-1}{2}}e^{-{\rm i}n^{2}(k-k_{0})\cdot\zeta}\widehat{\varphi}(n(k-k_{0}))\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
(|k|^{2}-|k_{0}|^{2})\psi^{(w)}_{3}\end{pmatrix}

and, with the substitution
\kappa=n(k-k_{0})
,

\|\widetilde{L}(\omega)\xi_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}^{2}=c^{2}\|\psi_{3}^{(w)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}|\widehat{\varphi}(\kappa)|^{2}(2n^{-1}k_{0}\cdot\kappa+n^{-2}|\kappa|^{2})\,{\rm d}\kappa\to 0\quad(n\to\infty).

∎

4.2.1
Media Periodic in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}

Here we consider once more the example of periodic materials. In contrast to the case of radiation in the
x_{1}
-direction, we assume that
\perm(\cdot,\omega)
is periodic on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
as well as on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
for each
\omega\in D(\perm)
.
As in Definition
3.4
, the
discriminant corresponding to (
2.15
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
will be denoted by
D^{(v)}_{\pm}
and the one for (
2.16
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
by
D^{(w)}_{\pm}
.
We provide a representation of the sets
N_{k}^{(v)}
and
N_{k}^{(w)}
defined in (
4.35
) and (
4.36
), as well as the determinants
d
and
\widetilde{d}
from Definition
3.1
using this Floquet-Bloch theory notation. The basic idea is that a decaying solution
v
of the homogeneous equation exists on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
if
D_{\pm}^{(v)}\notin[-2,2]
. In this case the fundamental system
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
from (
2.35
) can be chosen as the fundamental system
v^{(1)}_{\pm}
and
v^{(2)}_{\pm}
in Definition
3.1
and in (
4.28
). This is because in this case
\mathop{\rm Re}(\kappa)>0
in (
2.35
). An analogous statement holds for the
w
-equation.

Lemma 4.14
.

Assume
\perm(\cdot,\omega)
is periodic on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
for each
\omega\in D(\perm)
and satisfies (
1.3
). Let
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
.

Corresponding to the periodic problem (
2.15
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
denote the discriminant by
D_{\pm}^{(v)}
and let
d(k)
be the quantity defined in Definition
3.1
.

Analogously, for (
2.16
) on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
, we define
D_{\pm}^{(w)}
and use
\tilde{d}(k)
from Definition
3.1
.

Then, with
N^{(v)}_{k}
and
N^{(w)}_{k}
defined as in (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) (with
k_{0}
replaced by
k
), respectively,

N^{(v)}_{k}=\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega:D^{(v)}_{+},D^{(v)}_{-}\not\in[-2,2]\ \text{and}\ d(k)=0\ \text{holds}\},

N^{(w)}_{k}=\{\omega\in D(\perm):D^{(w)}_{+},D^{(w)}_{-}\not\in[-2,2]\ \text{and}\ \widetilde{d}(k)=0\ \text{holds}\}.

Proof.

As follows from the Floquet-Bloch theory, we get fundamental systems satisfying (
4.28
) if and only if

D^{(v)}_{+},D^{(v)}_{-}\not\in[-2,2]

(4.67)

or

D^{(w)}_{+},D^{(w)}_{-}\not\in[-2,2].

(4.68)

Note that (
4.31
) and
d(k)=0
are the same - and similarly for (
4.34
) and
\tilde{d}(k)=0
.

∎

4.2.2
Media Homogenous in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}

Here we study once more the special case
\perm_{\pm}(x,\omega)=\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
for
\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0}
, where
\Omega_{0}
is defined in (
1.4
).
Recall the sets
N^{(v)}_{k}
and
N^{(w)}_{k}
defined in (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) (each with
k_{0}
replaced by
k
), respectively, and
N^{(v)}
given by (
4.41
).

Lemma 4.15
.

We consider the sets

\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0}:\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)\neq 0,\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}^{2}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)},\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{-}^{2}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}\notin[0,\infty),\right.

(4.69)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)}{\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)}\in(0,\infty)\right\}

and

\displaystyle N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}=

\displaystyle\left\{\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0},|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega),\ |k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(\omega)\notin(-\infty,0],\right.

(4.70)

\displaystyle\left.\ \text{and}\ \omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)=|k|^{2}(\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega))\right\}.

We have

\displaystyle N^{(v)}_{k}

\displaystyle=N_{k}^{\mathrm{red}}\quad\forall k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\setminus\{0\},

(4.71)

\displaystyle N^{(v)}_{0}

\displaystyle=\emptyset,

(4.72)

\displaystyle N^{(w)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\emptyset\quad\forall k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}.

(4.73)

Moreover,
N^{(v)}={\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})\setminus\Omega_{0}
.

Proof.

For
\omega\in D(\perm)\setminus\Omega_{0}
the homogenous form of (
2.15
) reduces to

-v^{\prime\prime}+(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm)v=0.

(4.74)

For
|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\in(-\infty,0]
, equation (
4.74
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
has only the trivial
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
solution, i.e. (
4.28
) fails. The same argument applies for
|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(\omega)\in(-\infty,0]
.

Assume
|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\omega)\not\in(-\infty,0]
. Then
v^{(1)}_{-}(x_{1})=c_{-}e^{\mu_{-}x_{1}}
and
v^{(2)}_{+}(x_{1})=c_{+}e^{-\mu_{+}x_{1}}
, with
\mu_{\pm}:=\sqrt{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm}(\omega)}
and
c_{\pm}\in\mathbb{C}
. Hence, condition (
4.28
) is satisfied. Equation (
4.31
) (for
k\neq 0
) is equivalent to

\perm_{+}(\omega)\mu_{-}+\perm_{-}(\omega)\mu_{+}=0,

which is equivalent to

\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)=|k|^{2}(\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega)),

see Remark 4.6 in
[
7
]
. This shows (
4.71
).

Next, we show
N^{(v)}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})\setminus\Omega_{0}
. Once again,

N^{(v)}=\cup_{k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}}(N^{(v)}_{k}\setminus\Omega_{0})\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})\setminus(\Omega\cup\Omega_{0})\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})\setminus\Omega_{0},

where the first inclusion follows by Theorem
4.13
.

To prove (
4.72
), note that equation (
4.31
) (for
k=0
) is
\mu_{+}+\mu_{-}=0
, which cannot hold since
\mathop{\rm Re}\mu_{\pm}>0
by the definition of
\sqrt{z},
for
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0].

The proof of (
4.73
) is completely analogous to (
4.72
) because (
2.16
) has the same form as (
4.74
) with
k=0
and (
4.34
) has the same form as (
4.31
) with
k=0
.

The equality
N^{(v)}={\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{red}}
follows from (
4.71
), (
3.42
) and the obvious fact that
N_{0}^{\mathrm{red}}=\emptyset
.
The inclusion
N^{(v)}\subset\sigma_{\mathrm{Weyl}}({\mathcal{L}})
follows from Theorem
4.13
.
∎

5
Non-Existence of Eigenvalues

Clearly, all gradient fields are in the kernel of the curl operator. Hence, if
\perm(\cdot,\omega)
vanishes on an open set
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
, then gradient fields
\nabla f
with a smooth
f
and
\mbox{\rm supp}(f)\subset B
satisfy
L(\omega)\nabla f=0
and
\nabla f\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
. This means that such
\omega
is an eigenvalue of infinite geometric multiplicity.

However, outside
\Omega
, we expect no eigenvalues due to the shift invariance of the problem along any direction in the
x_{\parallel}-
variables. In other words, there is no mechanism for a full localization in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
. Hence, we expect
\sigma_{p}({\mathcal{L}})\cap(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Omega)=\emptyset.
This statement remains a conjecture and will be addressed in a future paper.
Here we only show the non-existence of eigenvalues with finite geometric multiplicity.

Theorem 5.1
.

There are no eigenvalues of
{\mathcal{L}}
of finite geometric multiplicity.

Proof.

Let
u\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
be a solution of
L(\omega)u=0
for some
\omega\in D(\perm)
. Then, due to the shift invariance of
{\mathcal{L}}
in
x_{\parallel}
, also
u_{\xi}(x):=u(x+(0,\xi)),x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},
satisfies
L(\omega)u_{\xi}=0
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
.

The following proof of the fact that
E:=\{u_{\xi}:\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{\omega}
is infinite dimensional can be found also in
[
13
]
, see Theorem 6.10.1. Let us assume that
\dim E<\infty
. For a fixed
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
the operator
S:f\mapsto f_{\zeta}
is linear and satisfies
S:E\to E
. Hence,
S
has an eigenvalue
\lambda
and an eigenfunction
\varphi\in E
, i.e.,
\varphi(x+(0,\zeta))=\lambda\varphi(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
.

As

\|\varphi(\cdot+(0,\zeta))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}}=\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}},

and
S
is symmetric, it follows that
\lambda\in\{-1,1\}
. We obtain
\varphi(x+(0,\zeta))=\varphi(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, i.e.,
\varphi
is periodic or anti-periodic. This is a contradiction to
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
.
∎

5.1
Media Homogeneous in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}

Let us first recall that

\Omega_{0}=\{\omega\in D(\perm):\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)=0\quad\text{or}\quad\omega^{2}\perm_{-}(\omega)=0\}.

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume
\perm_{\pm}(\cdot,\omega)=\perm_{\pm}(\omega)
. Then
\sigma_{p}({\mathcal{L}})\setminus\Omega_{0}=\emptyset.

Proof.

Let
\omega\in\sigma_{p}({\mathcal{L}})\setminus\Omega_{0}
and
(u_{1},v,w)
be the Fourier transform (in the variables
x_{\parallel}
) of a corresponding eigenfunction. Note that
(u_{1},v,w)(k)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})^{3}
for almost all
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
and
{\rm meas}(\mbox{\rm supp}(u_{1},v,w))\neq 0
. Recall from Lemma
2.2
that
u_{1}=-\frac{{\rm i}|k|}{|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm}v^{\prime}
and for homogeneous
\perm_{\pm}
the components
v(k)
and
w(k)
satisfy the same type of equations
-\varphi^{\prime\prime}+(|k|^{2}-\omega^{2}\perm_{\pm})\varphi=0
on
\mathbb{R}_{\pm}
. As explained in the proof of Lemma
4.15
, the condition
\llbracket w\rrbracket=\llbracket w^{\prime}\rrbracket=0
and condition (
4.34
) together with
w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, imply
w=0
.

For the
v
-component we saw in the proof of Lemma
4.15
that

v(x_{1},k)=\begin{cases}c_{+}(k)e^{-\mu_{+}(k)x_{1}},&x_{1}>0,\\
c_{-}(k)e^{\mu_{-}(k)x_{1}},&x_{1}<0\end{cases}

with
\mathop{\rm Re}\mu_{\pm}>0
and
c_{\pm}:\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\to\mathbb{C}.
The interface conditions for
v
and
u_{1}
are equivalent to

\omega^{2}\perm_{+}(\omega)\perm_{-}(\omega)=|k|^{2}(\perm_{+}(\omega)+\perm_{-}(\omega))

(5.1)

as shown in the introduction to Sec.
4.2
and the proof of Lemma
4.15
. Equation (
5.1
) is satisfied at almost every
k\in\mbox{\rm supp}(c_{+})\cup\mbox{\rm supp}(c_{-})
. However, because for
\omega\notin\Omega_{0}
equation (
5.1
) holds only for two values of
k\in\mathbb{R}
if
N=2
and for
k
on a circle in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
if
N=3
, i.e., on a set of measure zero, we get a contradiction with
(u_{1},v,w)
being non-null.
∎

Appendix A
Integration by Parts for
\nabla\times

The following two results are possibly well known but for readers’ convenience we provide them including the proofs.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
N=2,3
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}(\nabla\times u)\cdot\overline{v}\,{\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}u\cdot\overline{(\nabla\times v)}\,{\rm d}x\quad\text{ for all }\quad u,v\in H(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N}):=\{f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}:\nabla\times f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}\},

where we recall that
\nabla=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})^{\top}
if
N=3
and
\nabla=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},0)^{\top}
if
N=2
.

Proof.

For any open Lipschitz set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
we define

\Curl_{0}:H_{0}(\Curl,\Omega)\subset L^{2}(\Omega)^{3}\to L^{2}(\Omega)^{3},u\mapsto\nabla\times u,

where

H_{0}(\Curl,\Omega):=\overline{C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega)^{3}}^{H(\Curl,\Omega)},

i.e. with the closure with respect to the graph norm of
\Curl
. Then one has
\Curl=\Curl_{0}^{*}
and hence, automatically,

\langle\Curl_{0}u,v\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=\langle u,\Curl v\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\quad\forall u\in H_{0}(\Curl,\Omega),v\in H(\Curl,\Omega).

Lemma
A.2
shows that
H_{0}(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N})=H(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N})
and thus the statement follows.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
N=2,3
. Then

H_{0}(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N})=H(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N}).

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that for showing
H^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{N})=H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
.
Clearly,
H_{0}(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N})\subset H(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N}).
For the other direction let
u\in H(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N})
be given. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
we define

u^{(n)}:=(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u)\theta_{n}.

Here
\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}:=n^{N}\eta(n\cdot),

\eta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B_{1}(0)),\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\eta(x)\,{\rm d}x=1
generates a Dirac sequence
(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}})_{n}
and
\theta_{n}(x):=\theta(\tfrac{|x|}{n})
is a cut-off function with
\theta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
\theta(s)=1
for
|s|\leq 1,
and
\theta(s)=0
for
|s|\geq 2
. Because
(u^{(n)})_{n}\subset C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
, it remains to prove that
u^{(n)}\to u
in
H(\Curl,\mathbb{R}^{N})
. We have

\|u^{(n)}-u\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u-u)\theta_{n}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\theta_{n}u-u\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u-u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\theta_{n}u-u\|_{L^{2}}\to 0\quad(n\to\infty),

(A.1)

where the convergence of the first term is shown, e.g., in Theorem 2.29 of
[
1
]
and the convergence of the second term is obvious.
For
N=2
, to show
\|\nabla\times u^{(n)}-\nabla\times u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\to 0
it suffices to show that

\|\partial_{2}u_{3}^{(n)}-\partial_{2}u_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\to 0
and similarly for
\partial_{1}u_{3}
and
\partial_{2}u_{1}-\partial_{1}u_{2}
. For
N=3
we need to show
\|\partial_{2}u_{3}^{(n)}-\partial_{3}u_{2}^{(n)}-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\to 0
and similarly for
\partial_{3}u_{1}-\partial_{1}u_{3}
and
\partial_{2}u_{1}-\partial_{1}u_{2}
.

Let us show in detail
\|\partial_{2}u_{3}^{(n)}-\partial_{3}u_{2}^{(n)}-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\to 0
. All other convergences are analogous.
Using
\partial_{j}u^{(n)}=(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u)\partial_{j}\theta_{n}+(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*\partial_{j}u)\theta_{n}
, this follows from

\displaystyle\|\partial_{2}u_{3}^{(n)}

\displaystyle-\partial_{3}u_{2}^{(n)}-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u_{3})\partial_{2}\theta_{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u_{2})\partial_{3}\theta_{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\left(\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\right)\theta_{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\|(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\theta_{n}-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{n}\sum_{j=2}^{3}\|\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*u_{j}\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\eta_{\frac{1}{n}}*(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\theta_{n}-(\partial_{2}u_{3}-\partial_{3}u_{2})\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\to 0,

where the convergence of the last two terms is analogous to (
A.1
).
∎

Appendix B
Trace Operators

The interface conditions (
1.15
) and (
1.19
) were formulated using trace operators, which we define below. First, for an open set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
we use the standard notation

\Hdiv(\Omega):=\{E\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{3}:\nabla\cdot E\in L^{2}(\Omega)\},\quad\Hcurl(\Omega):=\{E\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{3}:\nabla\times E\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{3}\},

where we recall that
\nabla=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},0)^{\top}
if
N=2
and
\nabla=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})^{\top}
if
N=3
. We also define
\Gamma:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:x_{1}=0\}
and
\nu:=\rm{e}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, i.e., the unit normal on
\Gamma
pointing outward from
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{-}
. The classical trace operator

T_{0}:H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{m}\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{m}\quad(\text{such that }T_{0}f=f|_{\Gamma}\text{ for }f\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{m})

with
m\in\mathbb{N}
is used below. It is known that
T_{0}
is continuous and surjective, see Theorem 7.39 and Sec. 7.67 in
[
1
]
.

The trace operators
T_{\pm}^{n}
and
T_{\pm}^{t}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
used in (
1.15
) were defined in
[
7
]
. The definition in
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\pm}
, i.e. for
N=3
, is completely analogous. In summary, we have for
N=2,3

T^{n}_{\pm}:\Hdiv(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})\to H^{-1/2}(\Gamma),T^{n}_{\pm}E[\varphi]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}}E\cdot\nabla\tilde{\varphi}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}}\tilde{\varphi}\nabla\cdot E\,{\rm d}x\qquad\forall\varphi\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma),

where
\tilde{\varphi}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
is such that
T_{0}\tilde{\varphi}=\varphi
. The space
H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)
is the dual space of
H^{1/2}(\Gamma)
.

Similarly,

T^{t}_{\pm}:\Hcurl(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})\to(H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{3})^{\prime},T^{t}_{\pm}E[\Phi]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}}E\cdot\nabla\times\tilde{\Phi}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}}\tilde{\Phi}\cdot\nabla\times E\,{\rm d}x\qquad\forall\Phi\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{3},

where
\tilde{\Phi}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{3}
is such that
T_{0}\tilde{\Phi}=\Phi
.

Trace Operators and the Fourier Transform

The trace operators
\widehat{T_{\pm}^{n}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}
used in (
1.19
) are defined via

\widehat{T^{n}_{\pm}}:{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\Hdiv(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})\to{\mathcal{F}}_{t}H^{-1/2}(\Gamma),u\mapsto\widehat{T^{n}_{\pm}}u:={\mathcal{F}}_{t}(T^{n}_{\pm}E),

where
u={\mathcal{F}}_{t}E
with
E\in\Hdiv(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
. This definition clearly satisfies

\widehat{T^{n}_{\pm}}({\mathcal{F}}_{t}E)={\mathcal{F}}_{t}(T_{\pm}^{n}E)\qquad\forall E\in\Hdiv(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}).

For
E\in C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
we have
\widehat{T^{n}_{\pm}}u(k)=\mp\nu\cdot u(0,k)=\mp u_{1}(0,k)
for all
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
, where again
u={\mathcal{F}}_{t}E
. This follows from the fact that
T^{n}_{\pm}E=\mp E_{1}|_{\Gamma}
(shown by the divergence theorem, see
[
7
]
) and from
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}(E_{1}|_{\Gamma})(k)=u_{1}(0,k)
.

Note that elements of
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)
act on test functions
\varphi\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{-1}H^{1/2}(\Gamma)
. For
\varphi={\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{-1}\psi
with
\psi\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma)
we have

\widehat{T^{n}_{\pm}}u[\varphi]={\mathcal{F}}_{t}(T^{n}_{\pm}E)[{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{-1}\psi]=T_{\pm}^{n}E[\psi].

Similarly,
\widehat{T_{\pm}^{t}}
are defined via

\widehat{T^{t}_{\pm}}:{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\Hcurl(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})\to({\mathcal{F}}_{t}H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{3})^{\prime},u\mapsto\widehat{T^{t}_{\pm}}u:={\mathcal{F}}_{t}(T^{t}_{\pm}E),

where
u={\mathcal{F}}_{t}E
with
E\in\Hcurl(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
. We have

\widehat{T^{t}_{\pm}}({\mathcal{F}}_{t}E)={\mathcal{F}}_{t}(T_{\pm}^{t}E)\qquad\forall E\in\Hcurl(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})

and for
E\in C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm}})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{N}_{\pm})
we get
\widehat{T^{t}_{\pm}}u(k)=\mp\nu\times u(0,k)=\mp(-u_{2}(0,k),u_{1}(0,k),0)^{\top}
for all
k\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}
, where again
u={\mathcal{F}}_{t}E
.

Elements of
({\mathcal{F}}_{t}H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{3})^{\prime}
act on test functions
\Phi\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{-1}H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{3}
. For
\Phi={\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{-1}\Psi
with
\Psi\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma)^{3}
we have

\widehat{T^{t}_{\pm}}u[\Phi]={\mathcal{F}}_{t}(T^{t}_{\pm}E)[{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{-1}\Psi]=T_{\pm}^{t}E[\Psi].
